Repository evidence only, because each needs a fault the live host will not produce on demand: **C8-F1**'s dropped-node path and **C8-F2**'s `PAGE_SCAN_FAILED` both require a page that fails, **C8-F6** requires a detached PAGE, **C8-F7** requires a hostile thrown value, and **C8-F8** is a type-level change. C8-F3's *timeout* behaviour likewise rests on the coordinator's own timeout regressions plus a source-level assertion that `connectHandlers` routes through it, because `getConnectPayload` imports plugin main and is not directly invocable from the harness — the pre-existing §3a static-analysis boundary, not a new gap.
- **Operational note: a rebuild drops the bound plugin peer.** In both the `8mvc` and `zhfj` sessions the peer disconnected immediately after `bun run build:all`, at the same point in the sequence — Figma reloads a plugin whose `code.js` changes underneath it, which tears down the socket. Live verification and rebuilding therefore cannot be interleaved: a fix found live needs a fresh session to replay. The `r3yk` session confirmed the diagnosis by rebuilding nothing and running to a clean close. **Corrected 2026-07-29 (Change 9 review session):** the rule as first written — "verify the on-disk bundle by reading it, never by running `check:plugin`" — was necessary but not sufficient, and was disproved by following it. Running `bun run build:all` and the gate suite *before* joining channel `f3y7` still knocked the waiting plugin off the channel, because Figma reloads on the file write itself, not on a content change (`check:plugin` reported the bundle already up to date). The working order is therefore: **run the live session first, and run any build or gate that writes `figma_plugin/code.js` only after it has closed.** A rebuild is unsafe whenever the plugin is open, before or after connecting.
- **Document state.** No artifact remains, and this is now confirmed by a closing read rather than inferred. The `8mvc` probe collection, the `zhfj` probe frame, and the `r3yk` probe paint style were each created and deleted with the deletion confirmed, every `variable_delete` probe refused with zero mutation, and nothing else was written in any session. The `r3yk` session closed at **59 / 31 / 2** page descendants, **12** collections, and **10** variables — the same baseline all four sessions opened on. The `8mvc` and `zhfj` sessions ended without a closing read because the rebuild dropped the peer first; their state is confirmed transitively by this one. Each disconnect was itself clean D13 behaviour: the next tool call returned `PLUGIN_DISCONNECTED` naming the rejoin requirement, and a rejoin attempt correctly returned `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE` with `peerCount: 0`.

### Deliberately not addressed here

- **Playbook entries for the D9 codes**, including the two this change adds, are Phase 12 work and are listed there. No Phase 10 code has an `error-playbook.md` row yet; that is scheduled, not missing.
- **Per-command load bounds.** Q12 rejected total deadlines and concurrency caps as model-invisible. C8-D3 records the consequence rather than reversing the decision.

## Change 7: Phase 10 page-load isolation (Revs 59–60)

### Author: GPT-5.6 Sol (Extra High) @ 2026-07-28 5:40pm PT

**Release status (Rev 60, 2026-07-28): Phase 10 implementation, live correction, and verification are complete.** D14/Q12 now has one operational page-load policy across the ratified read and destructive-scan surfaces. A page that cannot be resolved, loaded, or traversed no longer erases successful data from other pages; every affected read returns the shared structured `coverage` contract, while commands scoped to one page throw the canonical page error directly. Destructive variable and collection deletion no longer treats a thrown page read—or an apparently empty collection—as evidence of zero consumers: incomplete coverage is an explicit `DOCUMENT_SCAN_INCOMPLETE` refusal before any removal. Live channel `08rz` then exposed and closed one MCP output-schema collision on the complete-scan, variable-in-use refusal path.

### Exact implementation contract

- **One per-command coordinator owns page resolution and loading.** `page_info(pageIds)`, `node_info` traversal, `component_list`, `variable_list` consumer scans, and `annotation_list` route page access through a cached coordinator. Each page contributes at most one ordered error row. Missing IDs, non-page targets, rejected loads, timed-out loads, and post-load traversal failures become canonical factory-built errors with page operands and diagnostic details.
- **Coverage is common and mechanically constrained.** The five registered read tools advertise and return `coverage: {complete, pageErrors: [{pageId, error}]}`. Successful page data remains present when another page fails, and the plugin result plus the server-side Zod parse enforce `complete === (pageErrors.length === 0)`. Single-page component, variable-consumer, and annotation reads rethrow `PAGE_NOT_FOUND`, `TARGET_NOT_PAGE`, `PAGE_LOAD_FAILED`, or `PAGE_LOAD_TIMEOUT` unchanged instead of wrapping them in raw host prose. *(Corrected in place per the Q21 precedent — Change 8, C8-D1. This bullet originally claimed the "Zod/JSON Schema boundary" enforced the invariant. It did not: `.superRefine()` is dropped in the zod→JSON-Schema conversion, so the advertised schema carried no invariant at all. Change 8 re-expresses `pageCoverage` as a two-branch union so the claim becomes true at both levels, and adds required `pagesAttempted` per C8-F4.)*
- **The Q12 timeout is bounded behavior, not a public tuning contract.** Production uses one internal 10-second timeout per `PageNode.loadAsync()` attempt. Tests can inject a shorter internal interval, but no MCP argument can alter it. The coordinator closes settlement when the timer wins; a later fulfillment or rejection is inert, cannot mutate the already-returned coverage, cannot trigger traversal, and cannot authorize a later destructive step in the same command. *(Change 8, C8-D3: the bound is **per page**, not per command. `page_info`, `component_list`, and `variable_list` iterate pages sequentially, so N failing pages cost N × the bound; only `variable_delete`'s scan is concurrent. Q12 rejected total deadlines deliberately, so this is a recorded consequence rather than a defect.)*
- **Destructive scans fail closed before mutation.** `variable_delete` resolves and validates its targets, starts the style/alias checks and one bounded node-consumer scan per document page, waits for all page attempts, then evaluates coverage before constructing deletion eligibility. Any load or traversal error raises `DOCUMENT_SCAN_INCOMPLETE` with the full failing-page coverage and reaches neither `Variable.remove()` nor `VariableCollection.remove()`. This ordering applies to variable mode, non-empty collection mode, and apparently empty collections; a fully scanned empty collection remains deletable.
- **The emitted bundle and agent-facing contract are synchronized.** The plugin refusal factories now carry page IDs, resolved non-page types, timeout values, causes, and incomplete-coverage details. Registered tool descriptions explain partial coverage and direct single-page errors, and the rebuilt `figma_plugin/code.js` contains the same coordinator and fail-closed gate as the TypeScript source.
- **Rev 60 preserves both `variable_delete` refusal shapes at the official SDK boundary.** A thrown D9 refusal uses the shared `{error: {code, message, details?}}` envelope, while a completed consumer scan that finds an in-use variable legitimately returns `{success:false, error:string, variablesInUse}`. The central output-schema wrapper now unions a tool's declared `error` type with the D9 envelope instead of overwriting it; `variable_delete` declares its refusal string explicitly. This prevents the client-side `-32602` that the first live deletion probe reproduced. *(Superseded by Change 8, C8-C1. Unioning a string into `error` removed the crash but left `error` with two possible types on one tool — the only place in the surface where a caller had to test the type of a field before reading it. The in-use refusal is now the coded `VARIABLE_IN_USE` with its consumers in `details.variablesInUse`, and the advertised string is retired. The wrapper's union mechanism is retained as a forward guard for any future tool that legitimately declares `error`, but no current tool does.)*

### Verification

- **Focused Phase 10 boundary matrix:** **94/94 tests with 872 assertions** across `v2.3.3.phase10.test.ts`, `mcpBoundary.test.ts`, and `outputSchema.test.ts`. The matrix covers successful-page retention beside load failure, missing/non-page resolution, all five advertised coverage schemas, direct single-page codes through the official SDK client, timeout and late settlement, post-load traversal failure, variable deletion, non-empty collection deletion, apparently empty collection deletion, the successful fully scanned control, and both `variable_delete.error` shapes through Zod, advertised JSON Schema, and the official SDK client.
- **Full repository suite:** **1,009/1,009 tests with 5,421 assertions across 51 files**. The socket-bearing suite was run with localhost binding available; the initial restricted-sandbox `EADDRINUSE`/startup failures were environmental and disappeared on that required rerun.
- **Build and static gates:** `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` pass. The synchronized version remains `2.3.2` as expected before Phase 13.
- **Live evidence — channel `08rz`, dedicated *MCP Test* file:** the official SDK joined a page-scoped `2.3.2` plugin/server pair exposing 46 tools and three pages. An early detailed `page_info` call reproduced real host `PAGE_LOAD_FAILED` rows for Pages 2 and 3 while retaining Page 1; the final matrix loaded all three pages and retained their data through `page_info`, `node_info`, `component_list` (8 components), and `variable_list`. The missing ID `999999:999999` returned `PAGE_NOT_FOUND`; component `42:6` returned `TARGET_NOT_PAGE`. A complete document scan found one fills consumer for `VariableID:1:12` and the post-fix in-use `variable_delete` refusal crossed the official SDK boundary without `-32602`. No Figma mutation committed: Page 1 remained **59→59 descendants**, local variables **10→10**, and collections **12→12**.
- **Evidence boundary:** the live run proves public discovery, partial `page_info` retention under a real transient host load failure, complete multi-surface reads, direct missing/non-page codes, the complete-scan in-use refusal, and unchanged document baselines. `PAGE_LOAD_TIMEOUT`, late settlement, an injected post-load traversal failure, and `DOCUMENT_SCAN_INCOMPLETE` before deletion remain deterministic repository evidence because manufacturing those paths against the live file would not add trustworthy coverage. *(Change 8, C8-D6: apparently-empty-collection deletion was originally listed here too, but it needs no fault injection — Change 8 verified it end to end on channel `sx3b`.)*

## Change 6: Phase 9 closure correction (Rev 58)

### Author: GPT-5.6 Sol (Ultra) @ 2026-07-28

**Release status (Rev 58, 2026-07-28): implementation, contract synchronization, and repository verification are complete.** This review audited the latest Change 5 commit (`ceaef0488e7cd5517c91fcbe95c5d1186a982740`) against its source, registered callbacks, real-WebSocket tests, generated artifacts, and live `rhn9` evidence. Its ledger contains **six functional gaps, seven testing gaps, and five documentation errors**. Three Change 5 closures were incomplete: P9-F2 covered only the socket-join leg, P9-F4 reclaimed an abandoned route only when a later command happened to dispatch, and P9-F7 distinguished a leave failure from a join timeout but gave recovery for a channel state the client no longer held. Final audits of the Change 6 refactor also found explicit structured `UNKNOWN_ERROR` was being double-prefixed, adding `releasedChannel` could discard contract-valid non-record origin details, and origin details on either join leg could fabricate predecessor state when the attempt had released nothing. Change 6 corrects all six behaviors, the missing adverse-path tests, and the documentation drift that allowed the overclaims.

| ID | Classification | Status | Finding and correction |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| C6-F1 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | Change 5's released-channel evidence was local to `joinChannel`. When the socket admitted the requested channel but the following `get_connect_payload` leg threw or returned a structured scope error, tool cleanup detached the new binding and lost the healthy predecessor. A failed same-channel rejoin also suppressed the evidence, while an already-invalidated binding could be misdescribed as healthy. `joinChannel` now exposes internal-only attempt metadata for an actually healthy predecessor, including a same-channel rejoin, through its success/error paths; `channel.ts` carries it through thrown and structured leg-2 failures, merges it into the originating details/message, and passes it into cleanup so the following `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` remains actionable. Invalidated bindings do not qualify, and successful public output does not expose the internal field. |
| C6-F2 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | Change 5 stamped pending routes but pruned them only during a later MCP dispatch, so an idle final request could still retain its route for the binding's lifetime and a late reply could still be forwarded before any sweeper command. Every route now owns an inactivity timer. Progress refreshes both its activity stamp and timer; terminal delivery, replacement, pair teardown, and server close cancel it. Plugin-frame arrival independently enforces the idle boundary, so a delayed host timer cannot let expired progress or a terminal reply revive the route. Dispatch pruning remains only a defensive backstop. |
| C6-F3 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | On `leave-previous-channel`, `resetChannel` had already cleared local state and closes its bridge socket when leave acknowledgement cannot be confirmed, but the tool and playbook said the previous channel was still held. Classification now distinguishes **code presence** from code value: any explicit string code, including `UNKNOWN_ERROR`, preserves its originating message verbatim; only a failure with no code receives the local fallback wording. These sections now state the origin-scoped twenty-one-code inventory and current Phase 9 contract. |

It now identifies the plugin-side ambiguity boundary and records the Rev 57/58 closure work and adverse-path matrix. |

The operator delivery boundary and truthful reconnect/rejoin flow are now explicit. |
| C6-D5 | Documentation error | **Corrected** | Plugin-source comments still said all ten Phase 9–11 operational codes lived in plugin `REFUSALS`. They now reserve that registry for plugin-origin D5/D6/D10 and Phase 10–11 codes and point socket/client codes to their actual origin registries. |

### Exact implementation contract

- **Both join legs preserve recovery evidence without reclassifying, erasing, or fabricating the origin.** Releasing a healthy binding is attempt metadata, not a public success field. A cross-channel switch and a same-channel rejoin both count because both release a usable reservation before replacing it. An invalidated binding does not count. `figma-client` stores its locally decided predecessor evidence separately from public error details; the registered tool passes that authoritative attempt context on the wire-join and scope-payload legs, including authoritative absence. If either leg fails, the originating code/message/details survive and gain a top-level `releasedChannel` only when that context says one was actually released; cleanup retains it for the following coded `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND`. Transport/plugin origin details are never authoritative. A same-named origin key is a reserved-key collision, remains nested under `originDetails`, and cannot mint a suffix, top-level recovery field, or later predecessor state. An explicit code is authoritative even when its value is `UNKNOWN_ERROR`, so its authored message is not given the fallback prefix; only an absent code triggers local fallback wording. A plain record without a collision gains the authoritative field directly; absent details become `{releasedChannel}` when recovery evidence exists; non-record details and colliding records remain byte-for-value under `originDetails` beside any authoritative field. A successful scope read completes the switch and omits the internal metadata from the tool result.
- **Route expiry is time-driven and checked on arrival.** Each pending route has one timer based on inactivity, not total age. Progress is liveness evidence and re-arms it. A route is deleted and its timer cleared on terminal response, request-ID replacement, MCP/plugin detach, or server close. Expired frames are rejected even if the runtime delivers the timer late; a later MCP command is not required for reclamation.
- **Leave failure is unbound recovery.** `resetChannel` clears local binding state before awaiting leave. The bridge unbinds before acknowledging, and the client closes its bridge socket when acknowledgement cannot be confirmed. `CHANNEL_JOIN_FAILED` with `phase: "leave-previous-channel"` therefore never tells an agent the previous channel is still held; it identifies both channel names and instructs an explicit join after automatic bridge reconnect.
- **The code inventory is origin-scoped.** The four D13 admission codes live in socket `CHANNEL_REFUSALS`; client-origin `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` lives in `CLIENT_REFUSALS`; the six Phase 10–11 operational codes and D5/D6/D10 verification codes live in plugin `REFUSALS`. The release inventory is eleven operational plus ten verification codes — **twenty-one codes plus `UNKNOWN_ERROR`** — with exactly one factory at the layer that can raise each code.

### Evidence boundary

- **Pre-Change-6 repository baseline:** the audited Rev 57 tree passed **976/976 tests with 5,219 assertions across 50 files**. The corrected seven-file Phase 9 matrix passed **115/115 with 812 assertions**. These numbers describe the audited baseline, not the unverified Change 6 edits.
- **Pre-Change-6 live baseline — channel `rhn9`:** 24/24 checks crossed the registered MCP boundary against the dedicated *MCP Test* document. The session returned the `2.3.2` pair and all 46 tools; raw peers proved mandatory `clientType`, unique peer IDs, `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS`/`CHANNEL_IN_USE` non-admission, and no pair traffic to refused peers. The registered tool proved the original P9-F2 **leg-1** disclosure, coded follow-on block, and explicit rejoin. A disposable frame/star cycle proved stale-parent refusal and cleanup, returning Page 1 from 59 descendants to 59.
- **Not post-fix live evidence:** `rhn9` predates the Change 6 implementation. It does not prove leg-2 scope-failure propagation, autonomous timer expiry, or leave-acknowledgement failure; those paths require deterministic fault injection and remain repository-verification claims unless a later live run explicitly drives them.
- **Final repository evidence:** the reproducible seven-file Phase 9 matrix passes **130/130 tests with 914 assertions**: `socketPeerBinding`, `figmaClientPeerBinding`, `v2.3.3.phase9.ui`, `connectHandlers`, `figmaClientTransport`, `figmaClientJoinError`, and `outputSchema`. The full `src/mcp_server/tests` suite passes **991/991 tests with 5,321 assertions across 50 files**. This includes C6-F4–F6 and C6-T5–T7. `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` all pass.

## Change 5: Phase 9 adversarial review and live closure (Revs 56–57)

### Author: Claude Opus 5 (Ultracode) @ 2026-07-28 10:15am PT

**Historical status (Revs 56–57, 2026-07-28): Phase 9 was implemented, repository-verified, and live-verified against a real Figma plugin, but Change 6 later proved the P9-F2, P9-F4, and P9-F7 closure claims incomplete.** Change 4 delivered the D13/Q11 implementation and claimed repository evidence only. Change 5's adversarial review found **ten functional gaps, one testing gap, and three documentation errors**; seven functional gaps, the recorded testing gap, and the three original documentation errors closed as claimed, while the three rows marked below are superseded by Rev 58. The separate Phase 7–8 ledger is unchanged at **24 functional gaps, 15 testing gaps, 9 documentation errors, and 1 nitpick**; Change 5 reviews Phase 9 and does not recategorize those historical rows.

Change 4's own verification claims were re-derived and are accurate. The focused matrix reproduces at exactly **98/98** (**705 assertions**) over `socketPeerBinding`, `figmaClientPeerBinding`, `v2.3.3.phase9.ui`, `connectHandlers`, `figmaClientTransport`, `figmaClientJoinError`, and `outputSchema`; the full suite reproduces at **959/959** (**5,122 assertions across 50 files**). Change 4 did not enumerate the focused file set, which is corrected here (P9-D3).

The central correction is P9-F1. `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS` cannot reach an agent, because a channel holds one plugin slot and the second plugin is refused at its own join, so `channel_join` never observes two plugin peers. The implementation is correct and safer than the specification; the PRD's D13 text and Phase 14 live matrix were wrong to describe the refusal as reachable through `channel_join`, and are corrected in place per the Q21 precedent.

| ID | Classification | Status | Finding |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| P9-F1 | Functional gap | **Resolved — live verified** | `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS` is unreachable through `channel_join`; the PRD's D13 wording and Phase 14 live matrix required a two-plugin `channel_join` refusal that cannot occur. |
| P9-F2 | Functional gap | **Initial leg-1 fix live verified; full closure superseded by C6-F1** | A failed `channel_join` released the caller's healthy binding first, then reported only the new channel's failure; neither the refusal nor the follow-on block named the released channel. |
| P9-F3 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | The plugin registry defined four socket-origin D13 refusals with no plugin throw site, shipping dead message strings in `code.js` and a parity test guarding a mirror with no consumer. |
| P9-F4 | Functional gap | **Initial dispatch-pruning fix incomplete; superseded by C6-F2** | `channel.pending` route entries were removed only on a terminal reply or pair teardown, so a command that never received one leaked its route for the life of the binding. |
| P9-F5 | Functional gap | **Resolved — live verified** | `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS.details.peerCount` was the hardcoded literal `2` presented as an observed count, beside an accurate `peerCount: 0` on the sibling refusal. |
| P9-F6 | Functional gap | **Resolved — live verified** | `isKnownChannelVersion` normalized before deciding a version was known, but the mismatch test compared raw strings, so a padded self-report could be both known and spuriously unequal. |
| P9-F7 | Functional gap | **Initial cause split incomplete; superseded by C6-F3** | A failure leaving the previous channel surfaced as `CHANNEL_JOIN_FAILED` with recovery text naming a join-acknowledgement timeout, which is the wrong cause. |
| P9-F8 | Functional gap | **Resolved — verified** | `CHANNEL_NOT_FOUND` became unreachable from the socket but remained the playbook's documented guidance for a missing channel, while the four D13 codes had no entries. |
| P9-F9 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | The Q11 scope-ready wait had no timeout, so a lost acknowledgement left the plugin UI at "Connecting…" with no stated recovery. |
| P9-F10 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | `handleSocketMessage` deleted a pending request by an undefined `id` instead of `data.id`, a latent `ReferenceError` in the response path Phase 9 rewired. |
| P9-T1 | Testing gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | The peer-binding test was titled "without admitting the refused peer" but asserted only the returned codes; non-admission was never checked for the ambiguous or in-use cases. |
| P9-D1 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | The compatibility posture omitted that the socket now refuses any join without `clientType`, so an installed pre-2.3.3 plugin build cannot connect at all. |
| P9-D2 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | Change 4 justified the duplicated plugin-side D13 factories as "required by Q27's bundle boundary"; Q27 requires no plugin mirror of a code the plugin never throws. |
| P9-D3 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | Change 4's focused verification claim did not enumerate its file set, so the reported 98/705 was not directly reproducible. |

### Finding evidence and closure

- **P9-F1 (functional, high).** `ChannelState.plugin` is a single slot and `handlePluginJoin` refuses a second plugin at its own join, so `handleMcpJoin` has no ambiguity branch to reach. Live on `8vj3`: a probe peer joining as a second plugin received `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS` while the bound MCP session's `channel_join` kept succeeding throughout. The refusal is real and correct, but it is delivered to the joining plugin and rendered in the plugin UI, never to an MCP caller. D13, the Phase 14 matrix, and the code inventory now state the delivery boundary, and the playbook entry is written for the plugin operator rather than the agent.
- **P9-F2 (functional, high; initial closure superseded by C6-F1).** `joinChannel` calls `resetChannel()` before attempting a new join, because one socket connection owns at most one reservation. Live: with a healthy `8vj3` binding, `channel_join("zzz9")` returned `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE` and the next `page_info` returned `UNKNOWN_ERROR: Must join a channel before sending commands`. A mistyped four-character channel code therefore destroyed a working connection and reported neither that fact nor the name needed to restore it. Change 5 made that **wire-join (leg-1)** failure and the following block name the released channel. **Coded as `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` (Rev 57).** The first fix left the blocked state on the ratified `UNKNOWN_ERROR` fallback, which Q16 permits when the message carries the recovery. On review that was the weaker option: P9-F2 does not merely improve an existing message, it creates a *new reachable state* — before Phase 9, "no binding" could only mean "never joined", and a failed join could not spend a working one. D9's "adds or edits" rule therefore applies exactly as it did for `VARIABLE_SCOPES_MISSING` (Rev 27), the D6 parent pair (Rev 31), and `ANNOTATION_CATEGORY_NOT_FOUND` (Rev 46), and a state an agent must recover from earns a stable code and a playbook entry. Rev 57 made the inventory **twenty-one codes plus the fallback**. Change 6 later found that this evidence did not survive a successful wire join followed by a failing scope-payload leg, and that same-channel/invalidated cases were misclassified; C6-F1 is the full closure.
- **P9-F3 (functional, medium) and P9-D2 (documentation).** All four D13 messages shipped in `figma_plugin/code.js`, yet the only throw sites are in `src/socket.ts`. Q27's recorded rationale is that the plugin bundle cannot import `src/shared` at runtime, which justifies a separate registry for codes the plugin throws; it does not call for a copy of a socket-only code. The inventory is now split by origin: `SOCKET_OPERATIONAL_CODES` for the four D13 refusals and `PLUGIN_OPERATIONAL_CODES` for the Phase 10–11 entries. This split itself did not change the then-current twenty-code inventory; Rev 57 separately added client-origin `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND`, so the current contract is twenty-one codes plus the fallback.
- **P9-F4 (functional, medium; initial closure superseded by C6-F2).** Routes were set in `forwardCommand` and deleted only on a terminal frame or in `detachPlugin`/`detachMcp`. A client-side timeout, or a plugin that answers with progress and then stops, left the entry until the pair tore down. Change 5 added a last-activity stamp refreshed by progress and a dispatch-time pruner. Change 6 proved that this still retained an idle final request when no later MCP command arrived, then completed the contract with per-route timers and arrival-time expiry.
- **P9-F5 (functional, low).** Live output carried `"peerCount":2` for every ambiguous refusal regardless of how many plugins were involved, because the value was a literal in the factory. The field is removed; `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE` keeps `peerCount: 0`, which is an accurate observation of an empty channel.
- **P9-F6 (functional, low).** `isKnownChannelVersion` trims and lower-cases before deciding a value is known, while `handleMcpJoin` compared the raw strings. A self-report of `" 2.3.2"` would be treated as known and as unequal to `"2.3.2"`. Versions are now normalized once on ingest, so the known-check and the equality check operate on the same value.
- **P9-F7 (functional, low; initial closure superseded by C6-F3).** If the pre-join leave failed, the outer catch produced `CHANNEL_JOIN_FAILED`, whose recovery text stated that the plugin did not acknowledge the join in time. Change 5 tagged that failure with its phase and channel names, but its new branch incorrectly said the previous channel was still held. In fact `resetChannel` clears local state before awaiting leave and closes the bridge socket on an unconfirmed acknowledgement. Change 6 replaces the false retry premise with the explicit reconnect/rejoin flow.
- **P9-F8 (functional, low).** The socket replaced `CHANNEL_NOT_FOUND` with `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE` for a channel that does not exist, but the playbook still routed that case to the retired code and had no entry for any D13 refusal. The connection-error table now documents the four D13 codes with their recovery, and marks `CHANNEL_NOT_FOUND` as retired and no longer emitted by a current socket.
- **P9-F9 (functional, low).** Nothing bounded the wait between the UI posting `set-scope` and plugin main echoing `scope-ready`. The UI now abandons an unacknowledged request after a bounded interval and states the retry, and the acknowledgement remains one-shot so a late reply cannot open a socket for an abandoned request.
- **P9-F10 (functional, low).** `state.pendingRequests.delete(id)` referenced an undeclared binding where `data.id` was intended. The path is reachable only through the unused `sendCommand` helper, so it was latent rather than live, but it sits in the response handler Phase 9 rewired and is corrected.
- **P9-T1 (testing, medium).** The test asserted the refusal codes and stopped, so a regression that admitted the refused peer while still returning the right code would have passed under a title claiming otherwise. Non-admission is now proved behaviourally for both cases.
- **P9-D1 (documentation).** Live: a join frame without `clientType` receives `{"type":"error","message":"clientType must be exactly 'plugin' or 'mcp'."}`. An already-installed pre-2.3.3 plugin cannot connect to a 2.3.3 socket at all, which is a larger break than the listed `channel_join` field additions. The compatibility posture now records it.

### Resolution and verification

- The four D13 refusals live only in `src/shared/channelProtocol.ts`. `src/shared/errorCodes.ts` splits operational codes by origin, and the plugin registry holds exactly the codes the plugin can throw. A regression asserts both directions: the socket registry defines the four with recovery content, and the plugin registry does not define any of them.
- Change 5's `joinChannel` state covered the wire-join failure only. Change 6 carries an actually healthy predecessor as internal attempt metadata across both the wire join and the following scope-payload leg, including same-channel replacement and excluding an already-invalidated binding. Thrown and structured leg-2 failures merge that evidence into their message/details; cleanup preserves it for the following coded `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND`; successful output does not expose it.
- The `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` factory lives in a new `CLIENT_REFUSALS` registry in `channelProtocol.ts`, beside — not inside — the socket's `CHANNEL_REFUSALS`. This applies the P9-F3 rule a third time: a factory belongs where its code can be thrown, and this one is raised by the MCP client before any frame reaches the socket. Regressions assert one home per code and that neither the plugin nor the socket registry mirrors it. It carries no `Operation Denied:` prefix, matching `PLUGIN_DISCONNECTED`: a missing precondition is operational, and D9 reserves the prefix for policy and verification refusals.
- `PendingRoute` carries `lastActivityAt` and a cancellable per-route timer. Progress refreshes both; terminal delivery, replacement, detach, and server close clear the timer. Timer expiry reclaims a route without any later dispatch, while arrival-time enforcement drops expired progress/terminal frames even when a host timer fires late. Dispatch pruning remains defense in depth. The replacement regression proves autonomous reclamation without disturbing a progress-active neighbour.
- Version strings are normalized on ingest in both join paths, and the ambiguous refusal no longer reports a fabricated peer count.
- The plugin UI bounds its scope-ready wait and reports the retry; the stale-request guard is unchanged, so a late acknowledgement is still inert.
- `prd.md`, `task.md`, and `skills/figma-edit/references/error-playbook.md` are synchronized through Rev 58 by Change 6. `SAFETY.md` and the remaining guides stay on the Phase 12 sync schedule.
- Historical verification: Change 5's Rev 56 implementation reported **974/974** (**5,199 assertions across 50 files**). Rev 57's additional `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` work brought the audited pre-Change-6 baseline to **976/976** (**5,219 assertions across 50 files**) and the corrected seven-file Phase 9 matrix to **115/115** (**812 assertions**). Change 6's final reproducible seven-file matrix is **130/130 with 914 assertions**, and its full suite is **991/991 with 5,321 assertions across 50 files**.

### Live evidence — channels `8vj3` and `pt3j`

Live testing ran against the *MCP Test* document with the real plugin bound, starting and ending at **59 page descendants across 3 pages**. Fourteen raw-WebSocket protocol probes passed against the running bridge while the real MCP session stayed bound and functional, confirming distinct random peer IDs, all four refusals, cross-peer forgery rejection, terminal-replay suppression, acknowledged leave with reservation release, and post-teardown routing silence.

| Probe | Live result |
| :- | :- |
| `channel_join` on a bound channel | `serverVersion: "2.3.2"`, `pluginVersion: "2.3.2"` returned with the scope payload. |
| Second MCP peer on the live channel | `CHANNEL_IN_USE`; the real session's binding was unaffected. |
| Second plugin peer on the live channel | `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS` delivered to the probe, never to `channel_join` — P9-F1. |
| `channel_join` on an empty channel | `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE` with `errorDetails.peerCount: 0`. |
| `channel_join` against a plugin reporting `1.0.0` | `VERSION_MISMATCH` carrying both operands. |
| Forged reply from a refused peer to an in-flight request | Never delivered; the bound plugin's reply was. |
| Bound plugin disconnect | `PLUGIN_DISCONNECTED` with `pluginPeerId`; bridge reported the channel gone at `peerCount: 0`. |
| Command in flight at disconnect | Rejected in **1 ms** with `PLUGIN_DISCONNECTED`; nothing hung to the 30 s request timeout. |
| Fresh plugin present, no rejoin | Still `PLUGIN_DISCONNECTED` — the binding does not auto-heal. |
| Explicit `channel_join` after reconnect | Succeeded; reads and a write/delete cycle routed to the new peer. |

The in-flight measurement dispatched 53 commands through the real `figma-client.ts`; 45 completed before the drop and **one** was genuinely in flight when it happened. That single observation is unambiguous but is a sample of one, because the probe command returned faster than the dispatch interval. Deeper-backlog behaviour rests on the unit suite, not on this run.

Rev 57 covered the generic (no-`releasedChannel`) `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` factory through the direct-client harness. Change 6 later corrected the classification boundary: a failed same-channel replacement does carry the healthy channel it released, while a failed join from an already-invalidated binding remains generic because that attempt did not cost a usable connection.

**Not verified live.** The Change 6 fault paths remain repository evidence and are recorded rather than implied. **C6-F1's leg-2 case** requires a socket admission followed by an injected scope-payload failure. **C6-F2** requires deterministic expiry/late-frame control. **C6-F3** requires the leave leg itself to fail. Their registered-callback/client/real-WebSocket regressions are the closure evidence. **P9-F9** and the Q11 scope-ready ordering are plugin-UI behaviour, unreachable over MCP, and remain covered by the executable UI harness.

## Change 4: Peer-bound channel and scope-ready handshake (Rev 55)

### Author: GPT-5.6 Sol (Ultra) @ 2026-07-28 12:24am PT

**Release status (Rev 55, 2026-07-28): Phase 9 is implemented and repository-verified.** A channel is now an explicit binding between exactly one Figma plugin peer and one MCP session, rather than a broadcast room. The prior Phase 7–8 finding ledger remains unchanged at **24 functional gaps, 15 testing gaps, 9 documentation errors, and 1 nitpick**; Change 4 completes the separate D13/Q11 implementation phase and does not recategorize those historical review rows.

The Q11 source review confirmed the reported scope-ready race. The plugin UI posted `set-scope` and immediately constructed its WebSocket, while plugin main committed the scope in a separately delivered message and sent no acknowledgement. There was therefore no happens-before edge between permission commitment and advertising the plugin on the channel. The fix uses a one-shot request ID: plugin main echoes `scope-ready` only after all scope and asset-permission fields are committed, and the UI opens the socket only for the matching acknowledgement. Stray and duplicate acknowledgements are ignored.

### Implementation

- The plugin join frame now identifies `clientType: "plugin"` and carries the package-derived plugin build version. A coded join refusal remains visible in the UI after its socket closes.
- The socket bridge assigns every connection a random lifetime `peerId` and reserves each human channel name for one plugin plus one MCP peer. It refuses an MCP join with no plugin as `PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE`, a second plugin as `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS`, a second MCP as `CHANNEL_IN_USE`, and a known unequal self-reported version pair as `VERSION_MISMATCH`; refused peers are not admitted.
- Commands route only from the bound MCP to the bound plugin. Pending request correlation records both peer IDs; progress and terminal responses from another peer, stale responses after unbind, and terminal replays are ignored.
- `channel_join` now returns authoritative socket-acknowledged `serverVersion` and `pluginVersion` fields along with the editable-scope payload. The MCP server name/version and join self-report share one package-derived source, with no additional hard-coded release surface.
- `resetChannel` now sends and awaits a real socket `leave`, validates the `{left:true, channel}` acknowledgement, clears local binding state, rejects work from the old binding generation, and closes the socket if leave cleanup cannot be confirmed. A malformed admitted join acknowledgement is explicitly detached without allowing cleanup failure to replace the initiating error.
- When the bound plugin leaves or disconnects, the bridge tears down the pair and emits a structured invalidation to the MCP client. Pending commands are rejected and every non-join command is blocked locally and at the bridge until a fresh plugin and an explicit successful rejoin establish a new binding.
- The four D13 refusal code/message pairs initially had separate plugin and server factories, as then understood under Q27's bundle boundary, with an exact parity regression. **Superseded by Change 5 P9-F3/Rev 56:** these codes can originate only at socket admission, so the dead plugin mirror was removed. The socket executable's direct-entry check also handles npm/pnpm-style symlinked bin paths.

### Verification

- Focused Phase 9/client/channel/UI/transport matrix: **98/98** tests, **705 assertions**, including real localhost WebSocket integration, refusal-registry parity, malformed acknowledgement cleanup, scope-ready ordering, and the existing reconnect-loop regression.
- Full repository suite: **959/959** tests, **5,122 assertions across 50 files**. The first full run exposed one stale source-inspection oracle that still required `pkg.version` inside `server.ts`; it was corrected to verify the shared package-derived version module at source and runtime, after which the full suite passed.
- `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` pass. The rebuilt socket and MCP CLI entry points both report `2.3.2`; that synchronized version remains expected until the Phase 13 bump.
- The localhost tests cannot bind inside the managed sandbox (reported there as `EADDRINUSE` even for port `0`); the identical permitted run passed. This is repository integration evidence, not a Phase 14 live-Figma claim. No live document mutation was needed or performed for Phase 9, and the later release-wide live matrix remains open.

## Change 3: Name-assignment sink closure and contract sync (Rev 54)

### Author: GPT-5.6 Sol (Ultra) @ 2026-07-27 11:30pm PT

**Release status (Rev 54, 2026-07-27): Change 2's two repaired tools remain correct, but its claim to have closed the whole name-assignment class was false and is superseded here.** The follow-up review found three functional gaps, two testing gaps, and three documentation errors. The cumulative Phase 7–8 ledger is now **24 functional gaps, 15 testing gaps, 9 documentation errors, and 1 nitpick**. The count is mechanical: Change 1 recorded 20/12/6/1; Change 2 added F78-21 and T78-13, producing 21/13/6/1; Change 3 adds F78-22–F78-24, T78-14–T78-15, and D78-07–D78-09, producing 24/15/9/1. A row may cover multiple instances of the same root defect (F78-23 and D78-09 do), but it is counted once under its recorded ID.

All eight Change 3 findings are fixed in the current implementation and documentation. The repaired contract is action-sensitive: an explicitly empty value is refused whenever a field **assigns** a user-visible name, but omission is recommended only when that particular field is optional and omission has a defined default/no-change meaning. Required creation/rename fields instead instruct the caller to supply a non-empty value. Lookup and verification fields retain their own semantics. C9 established present-empty exactness for the two `parentNodeName` paths; it never established a universal rule for every field that can look up an object by name.

Repository verification is green at **941/941** (**5,005 assertions across 47 files**, including the out-of-sandbox localhost transport test); the focused five-file Change 3/Phase 4/8/safety/resources matrix is **181/181** (**1,205 assertions**). `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` all pass. `check:versions` correctly reports synchronized **2.3.2**, because the version bump remains a Phase 13 task. A documentation-contract rerun after the final wording changes is also green at **56/56** (**237 assertions across 2 files**), covering the bidirectional `SAFETY.md` gate matrix and exact served-guide source/semantic parity.

| ID | Classification | Status | Finding |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| F78-22 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified; pre-fix live reproduced** | `variable_manage CREATE_COLLECTION` accepted `modeName:""`, created the collection, silently retained native `Mode 1`, and reported success. |
| F78-23 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | The plugin trust boundary lacked pre-mutation empty-name backstops for `UPDATE_VARIABLE.name` and component-property EDIT `newPropertyName`, despite MCP refusals. |
| F78-24 | Functional gap | **Resolved — repository verified; pre-fix live reproduced** | Component-property ADD accepted `propertyName:""` at MCP, then failed at the plugin as a generic `UNKNOWN_ERROR` instead of an actionable field refusal. |
| T78-14 | Testing gap | **Resolved — repository verified** | The alleged class inventory was a self-referential positive list that omitted real assignment sinks and could not fail when another sink went unclassified. |
| T78-15 | Testing gap | **Resolved — repository and live verified** | The node rename/group refusal regression parsed schemas/private validation directly rather than crossing the official SDK boundary and asserting exact `-32602` behavior. |
| D78-07 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | Shared empty-name recovery told callers to omit a name even when the assigning field was required, notably `node_rename.name` and `style_manage.name` on CREATE. |
| D78-08 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | Change 2 generalized C9's parent-name exactness to unrelated lookup/assignment fields and therefore described the class boundary incorrectly. |
| D78-09 | Documentation error | **Resolved — verified** | Rev 53, the expanded ledger, public safety/guides, root changelog, and Change 1's orphan status were not synchronized. |

### Finding evidence and closure

- **F78-22 (functional, high).** `variable_manage.modeName` was an unconstrained optional string, and the CREATE_COLLECTION handler created the collection before `if (modeName)` silently skipped `""`. The `9sxx` call returned success with native `Mode 1`. Closure requires an action-conditional MCP refusal and a plugin check before collection creation, while omission still returns the native default; both are now covered. The emitted `name`/`modeName` descriptions expose the same create/update/default distinction before the first call.
- **F78-23 (functional, high).** `UPDATE_VARIABLE.name:""` was already rejected by the MCP refinement, but a client bypassing Zod reached `if (name)` and received success without a rename. Component-property EDIT similarly forwarded `newPropertyName:""` to the host without a plugin check. Both handlers now refuse before lookup/mutation, with omission retaining each update's no-change behavior.
- **F78-24 (functional, medium).** Component-property ADD treated `propertyName` as bare `string`; the live empty call crossed MCP and then hit the handler's truthy required-field check, surfacing only generic `UNKNOWN_ERROR: Missing nodeId, action, or propertyName parameter`. ADD now has a field-path MCP issue and a plugin pre-mutation guard; EDIT keeps the same field as a lookup. The emitted `propertyName`/`newPropertyName` descriptions state those two action roles and their required/no-change behavior.
- **T78-14 (testing, high).** `NAME_ASSIGNMENT_FIELDS` was its own eight-entry expectation, so a new or forgotten sink did not affect the test. It already omitted collection `modeName` and ADD `propertyName`, while refinement-only entries were tested separately. Closure replaces completeness-by-assertion with an AST scanner for direct `.name` writes plus an explicit inventory of recognized Figma naming APIs, compared against a separately authored public action/field classification and internal/derived disposition. A red-proof temporarily added an unclassified direct `.name` sink, observed the inventory test fail, and then removed the probe.
- **T78-15 (testing, medium).** The advertised rename/group “boundary” regression invoked `safeParseAsync` and private `validateToolInput`; it did not call the tool through an official MCP client or assert the in-band `-32602` envelope. The new regression crosses the registered SDK boundary and pins the issue path/code. The `9sxx` client independently observed both refusals with zero mutation.
- **D78-07 (documentation, medium).** The shared helper always said “omit … to use the default name,” even for required `node_rename.name`; the style path likewise told a CREATE caller to omit its required `name`. Each refusal now receives action-specific recovery: node rename and style CREATE require a non-empty value, while style UPDATE may omit `name` to leave it unchanged. Every public guide distinguishes all required, native-default, and no-change omission branches, including the required variable-creation names.
- **D78-08 (documentation, medium).** Change 2 excluded broad “lookup fields” by claiming present-empty exact comparison “per C9.” The code and decision record support that edge rule for `parentNodeName` on two protected paths, not for every variable/style/property lookup; moreover, `propertyName` assigns during ADD. Rev 54 text classifies by role/action and makes no universal C9 claim.
- **D78-09 (documentation, medium).** Rev 53 stopped at this file: `prd.md`/`task.md` still reported Rev 52 and 20/12/6/1, the revision history ended at 52, the root changelog and public safety/guides named only the five creators, and Change 1 still called orphan `1396:123` current after Change 2 proved it absent. Rev 54 synchronizes every listed surface and labels the older sections/history honestly.

### Resolution and verification

- `variable_manage CREATE_COLLECTION.modeName` now rejects an explicit `""` at the emitted MCP schema and again in the plugin before `createVariableCollection`; omission keeps the native default mode. `UPDATE_VARIABLE.name` keeps its existing action-conditional MCP refinement and gains the missing plugin pre-mutation backstop; omission leaves the current variable name unchanged.
- `component_manage_property` classifies `propertyName` by action instead of treating the field as lookup-only: ADD requires a non-empty assigned property name at MCP and plugin boundaries, while EDIT continues to use `propertyName` as the existing-property lookup. EDIT's optional `newPropertyName` is guarded in the plugin as well as the MCP schema; omission leaves the property name unchanged.
- Empty-name recovery is now supplied by each call site. Required rename/create fields—including `node_rename.name`, style/variable CREATE `name`, and component-property ADD `propertyName`—say to supply a non-empty value; optional defaulting fields say to omit for the native default; optional style/variable/property update fields say to omit to leave the current name unchanged.
- The always-visible emitted field descriptions carry the same action-sensitive contract for `variable_manage.name`/`modeName`, `style_manage.name`, and component-property `propertyName`/`newPropertyName`. An official `tools/list` boundary regression pins that first-call guidance rather than inspecting only raw registration objects.
- The test oracle independently scans source-level direct `.name` writes and calls to an explicit set of recognized Figma naming APIs, then compares those results with the public-contract classification. It covers `create_shape.name`, `create_frame.name`, `create_text.name`, `create_svg.name`, `create_component_set.componentSetName`, `node_rename.name`, `node_group.name`, `style_manage.name`, `variable_manage.name` for CREATE_COLLECTION/CREATE_VARIABLE/UPDATE_VARIABLE, `variable_manage.modeName` for CREATE_COLLECTION, component-property ADD `propertyName`, and component-property EDIT `newPropertyName`. Internal/derived assignments are classified separately. A new direct write or use of a recognized naming API fails parity; a genuinely new Figma naming API must also be added to the scanner's method inventory.
- Official-SDK regressions now call the registered tools and assert the exact in-band MCP `-32602` issue path for node rename/group, rather than treating `safeParse` or private validation as an end-to-end boundary proof.
- `prd.md`, `task.md`, this revision history, the root `CHANGELOG.md`, `SAFETY.md`, and all four canonical `skills/figma-edit/references/*.md` sources are synchronized to Rev 54. Those Markdown sources are the bodies served by `figma-edit://guide/*`; there is no second resource-body copy to edit.
- Verification: focused five-file Change 3/Phase 4/8/safety/resources suites → **181 pass, 0 fail, 1,205 assertions**; `bun test src/mcp_server/tests` → **941 pass, 0 fail, 5,005 assertions, 47 files**. `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` pass; the final focused documentation-contract rerun is **56/56** with 237 assertions. The version gate's synchronized `2.3.2` result is expected before Phase 13.

### Live evidence — channel `9sxx`

The live pass used a real official MCP SDK `Client` with `StdioClientTransport` against the built stdio server and observed all **46** registered tools. The starting document state was **59 page descendants, 12 local variable collections, and 10 local variables**.

| Pre-fix probe | Live result |
| :- | :- |
| `node_rename({name:""})` | `-32602` at path `name`; target unchanged. |
| `node_group({name:""})` | `-32602` at path `name`; candidates remained ungrouped. |
| Component-property EDIT with `newPropertyName:""` | `-32602` at the exact field path; property unchanged. |
| Component-property ADD with `propertyName:""` | Passed the schema, then returned `UNKNOWN_ERROR` for the plugin's generic missing-parameter check; component unchanged. |

Node/component cleanup restored the page to its **59-descendant** baseline. This matrix is evidence of the pre-fix boundary and the already-correct node/refinement paths; it is not mislabeled as a post-fix live replay of F78-22–F78-24.

An additional **8/8** `9sxx` control pass established the neighboring whitespace semantics under independent readback: component-property ADD with `propertyName:" "` persisted one space; EDIT renamed it to `"  "` and persisted two spaces; `CREATE_COLLECTION.modeName:" "` returned and read back as exactly one space. The control collection and disposable root were removed, leaving **59 descendants / 12 collections / 10 variables**. This backs the exact-empty boundary without treating non-empty whitespace as omission.

**Historical environment interruption, resolved.** The first cleanup attempts for empty collection `VariableCollectionId:1413:35` (`Codex Change2 Variable Probe 9sxx`, **0 variable IDs**) hit the Figma host's 10-second connection timeout. A later official-SDK `variable_delete` retry on the same channel succeeded; `variable_list` returned no matching collection, counts returned to the original **12 collections / 10 variables**, and a fresh join still reported **59 page descendants**. No disposable artifact or cleanup blocker remains. The transient timeout is retained here because it explains the interrupted first collection attempt, not because it is a code finding.

After the final build, three consecutive official-SDK attempts to rejoin `9sxx` returned `CHANNEL_NOT_FOUND`; none reached a mutation. Post-fix live replay was therefore unavailable after the successful pre-fix/control/cleanup sessions. F78-22–F78-24 are closed by the repository and official-SDK boundary tests plus pre-fix live reproduction, not by a claimed post-fix live empty-name replay.

## Change 2: Name-assignment defect class review (historical; superseded by Change 3)

### Author: Claude Opus 5 (Ultracode) @ 2026-07-27 10:05pm PT

> **Historical status.** The `node_rename`/`node_group` repairs and their `a7ps`/`uy38` evidence remain valid, but Change 3 disproved this section's whole-class closure claim and supersedes its current-status/count statements.

**Historical Rev 53 status claim (2026-07-27): Change 1's verification claims reproduced, and F78-15 was believed to be extended from five instances to the whole defect class.** An independent review of `release-changelog.md` and commit `20e144c` re-ran every falsifiable Change 1 claim: the suite was **925/925** (**4,809 assertions across 47 files**) as stated, `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` passed, the official SDK registered **46** tools, and the then-current ledger counts matched its table. The schema-side repairs were present as described (five `minLength:1` name fields, the pinned 19-literal `BlendMode`, the live-confirmed numeric bounds), and `removeUncommitted` correctly required positive `removed === true` confirmation rather than trusting a non-throwing `remove()`. Change 3 preserves those reproduced facts while withdrawing the whole-class conclusion.

Live probing on channel `a7ps` then found that F78-15 had been closed **instance-by-instance rather than as a class**: two further tools that assign a user-visible name still substituted a Figma default silently and reported success. That is recorded here as **F78-21**, with the stub-only whitespace oracle recorded as **T78-13**. Both tools were fixed, but Rev 53's manual list did **not** pin the full class; Change 3 found its omitted sinks and replaced it with independent source-sink parity. Rev 53 repository verification was green at **932/932** (**4,839 assertions across 47 files**) with every recorded build/static gate passing; the post-fix live matrix on channel `uy38` passed **7/7**, with zero mutation confirmed after each refusal and the page restored to its 59-descendant baseline.

Whitespace normalization was tested live and correctly rejected as a finding: non-empty whitespace names are preserved by Figma. Rev 53 also observed that `component_manage_property.newPropertyName:""` failed at the host, but its conclusion that this removed the field from the assignment class was wrong: Change 3 records the missing plugin backstop under F78-23. One earlier item closed on inspection: F78-01's live orphan `1396:123` no longer exists in the test document.

| ID | Classification | Status | Finding |
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
| F78-21 | Functional gap | **Resolved — live verified** | The empty-name defect was closed by instance, so `node_rename` and `node_group` still substituted a Figma default silently and reported success. |
| T78-13 | Testing gap | **Resolved — live verified** | The whitespace-preservation oracle was stub-only, so it could not establish the host behaviour its title asserted. |

### Resolution and verification

- The `node_rename.name` and `node_group.name` portion of the name-assignment class is enforced at both boundaries. Both gain `min(1)` at the MCP schema and `assertNonEmptyExplicitName` at the plugin before any mutation; `groupNodes` moves from `if (name)` to `if (name !== undefined)`, the same truthiness correction F78-15 applied to the five creators. Change 3 completes the class inventory.
- `variable_manage`'s `CREATE_VARIABLE`/`CREATE_COLLECTION` branches reported a supplied-but-empty name as "name is required", conflating two causes against D9's distinct-cause rule. They now name the empty cause separately while keeping the missing-cause message intact.
- `component_manage_property.newPropertyName` gains `min(1)`, moving conforming-client refusal from one round trip later carrying raw host prose to the boundary with an actionable path. Rev 53 incorrectly inferred from the host refusal that the field was not a class member; Change 3 adds the missing plugin trust-boundary guard.
- Classification is by **action and role**, not merely by field spelling. C9 establishes present-empty exact comparison for `parentNodeName` on its two protected parent paths; it does not universalize that rule to every lookup field. A dual-role field stays a lookup where it identifies an existing object but joins the assignment inventory where it creates a name — notably component-property `propertyName` on ADD versus EDIT. Content fields (`text`, `labelMarkdown`) remain outside the name-assignment class and keep their own contracts.
- The shared helper is renamed `assertNonEmptyExplicitCreatorName` → `assertNonEmptyExplicitName` with **no alias retained** (the Q26 do-not-accrue-legacy rule), because it now guards the class rather than the creators alone.
- Rev 53's `NAME_ASSIGNMENT_FIELDS` positive list asserted `minLength: 1` for the eight fields it enumerated in emitted `tools/list`, but could not prove completeness and omitted real sinks. Its behavioral and plugin-level node tests remain useful evidence; Change 3 replaces the completeness claim with an independent source-sink/classification oracle.
- Repository verification: `bun test src/mcp_server/tests` → **932 pass, 0 fail, 4,839 assertions, 47 files**. `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, and `check:plugin` all pass.

### Functional gaps

#### F78-21 — the empty-name defect was closed by instance, not by class

**Evidence.** F78-15 fixed the five creator surfaces it enumerated. Live probing on channel `a7ps` found the identical defect on two further tools that assign a user-visible name, both reporting success:

| Call | Result |
| :- | :- |
| `create_shape({name: ""})` | `-32602`, `too_small, minimum: 1` — refused |
| `node_rename({name: ""})` | `{"name":"Rectangle","oldName":" "}` — **silently renamed, success** |
| `node_group({name: ""})` | `{"name":"Group","childCount":2}` — **silently ignored, success** |

Three tools, one document, one release, three answers to the same input. `node_rename` is the tool F78-15 used as its own discriminator to prove native normalization — the evidence was gathered there and the fix applied everywhere else. `node_group` discarded the value even earlier, through the `if (name)` truthiness check that F78-15 specifically replaced with presence-sensitive assignment in the five creators.

Under the Golden Rule this fails both halves. First-call correctness: the emitted schema advertised bare `string`, so a model composing from the schema alone reasonably sends `""`. One-round-trip recovery: there is no error, so there is no round trip — the model is never told anything happened, which is the same unrecoverable silence that decided Q35.

**Required closure.** Close the class rather than two more instances: inventory every field that ASSIGNS a user-visible name, apply one rule at both boundaries, and pin the inventory so a new tool cannot be added off-contract.

**Resolution.** See "Resolution and verification" above. Post-fix live matrix on channel `uy38` — **7/7**:

| Probe | Result |
| :- | :- |
| `node_rename({name: ""})` | refused, `too_small, minimum: 1` at `name` |
| `node_group({name: ""})` | refused, `too_small, minimum: 1` at `name` |
| `component_manage_property({newPropertyName: ""})` | refused at the boundary — previously a raw Figma error one round trip later |
| Zero mutation after both refusals | page held at 60 descendants; the two candidate `Star` nodes remained ungrouped at page level |
| `node_rename({name: "  "})` | applied and persisted — whitespace remains a real name |
| `node_group` with `name` omitted | `Group` — Figma's default, unchanged |
| `node_group({name: "Named Group"})` | applied — presence-based assignment works for supplied names |

Cleanup removed both disposable artifacts (`GroupProbe`, and the `PropProbe` component created for the `newPropertyName` probe) and the page returned to its 59-descendant baseline.

### Testing gaps

#### T78-13 — the whitespace oracle was stub-only

**Evidence.** `creatorValidation.ts` stated as fact that "Whitespace is an intentional, non-empty name and must not be normalized", and the supporting test — titled "accepts non-empty whitespace names **without normalization**" — drove local stubs and asserted the name read back as `" "`. That is the same evidentiary error T78-10 recorded for `""`: *"its Figma stubs allowed a node name to remain `\"\"` … the green matrix proved only the local stub behavior and could not establish the claimed contract."* The `4mor` matrix verified explicit-empty refusals and omission defaults; it never probed whitespace. The `""` boundary therefore rested on an untested assumption about where Figma's normalization begins.

**Required closure.** Establish the host behaviour live, then either keep the boundary at `""` with live backing or move it.

**Resolution.** Whitespace is **preserved**. On channel `a7ps`, `" "`, `"   "`, and `"\t"` all persisted exactly under independent `node_info` readback rather than the creating call's own echo. The `=== ""` boundary is therefore correct as written, and the claim is now host-verified rather than stub-inferred. The boundary suite additionally asserts that whitespace continues to pass the registered schema for every newly guarded tool, so a future tightening cannot silently swallow it.

### Live hypotheses rejected — not findings

- **Whitespace normalization.** Raised as a suspected F78-15-class defect; disproved live (see T78-13). Recorded because the concern was methodologically valid even though the conclusion was wrong — the fix needed live backing, not a different boundary.
- **`component_manage_property.newPropertyName` host substitution.** Figma rejects an empty property name outright (`in editComponentProperty: Must pass in valid name, defaultValue, preferredValues, description, or slotSettings`), so the narrower hypothesis that the host silently substitutes a property name was disproved. Rev 53's stronger conclusion that no trust-boundary finding existed was wrong; Change 3 records and fixes the missing plugin pre-mutation backstop under F78-23.
- **F78-01's live orphan.** Component `1396:123`, recorded in Change 1 as surviving in the test document, no longer exists (`missingNodeIds`). No cleanup action is outstanding.

**Release status (Rev 52, 2026-07-27): F78-15 is corrected, repository-verified, and live-verified.** Channel `2476` passed 16 of 21 pre-fix checks and reopened only F78-15; replacement channel `4mor` then passed all **10/10** post-fix functional probes: five explicit-empty refusals with zero descendant-count drift and five successful omission/default paths. Re-review of commit `e4e5b99`, deterministic adverse-path probes, the earlier live findings on Figma channel `o4g6`, two bounded independent adverse-path passes, and the completed `2476`/`4mor` runs found **20 functional gaps, 12 testing gaps, 6 documentation errors, and 1 nitpick**. F78-15 is a correction to an existing finding rather than a new ledger row: the Rev 51 preservation approach was disproved by the live host, so explicit empty creator/set names now fail closed before mutation while omission retains the existing defaults.
