### Resolution and verification

- Creator containment now refuses instance-interior component conversion before implicit creation, treats cleanup as successful only after positive removal evidence, and transports survivor location without masking the initiating error. Component conversion and component-set recovery use total, best-effort evidence collection and disclose every removed, relocated, unknown, or un-restored state.
- Annotation traversal accepts non-annotatable container roots. Effect schemas are strict per variant, enforce the live-confirmed numeric bounds, and share the exact pinned 19-literal `BlendMode` inventory. `create_instance.componentId` is now part of the advertised output contract.
- Independent test oracles pin the two catchalls, all eight creators and their output schemas, all 35 name-verification fields/read tools, all six ordinary creator cleanup call sites, all four served guides, and the exact NOISE error path. The two legacy fixtures uncovered by the final full-suite run now model real parent placement and host recovery evidence.
- Rev 51 closed the deeper recovery/totality pass for surviving component sets, tri-state removal/location evidence, hostile errors, annotation counts, and `create_text` validation. Rev 52 corrects its empty-name premise: the live host normalizes `""`, so all five affected surfaces reject an explicitly empty new name at both MCP and plugin boundaries before any creator, rename, or combine mutation; omission alone selects the established Figma/plugin default.
- Rev 52 repository verification: `bun test src/mcp_server/tests` → **925 pass, 0 fail, 4,809 assertions, 47 files**. `build:all`, `check:types:plugin`, `check:suppressions`, `check:generated`, `check:versions`, `check:plugin`, and `git diff --check` all pass. The bidirectional `SAFETY.md` parser treats the new explicit-name rule as a first-class gate for exactly the five affected tools.
- Live status: the official SDK client confirmed all 46 tools. On channel `2476`, the pre-fix disposable matrix passed **16/21** checks: instance-interior refusal with zero component-count drift, GROUP-root annotation traversal, strict effect variants/bounds/BlendMode plus valid GLASS, `create_instance.componentId`, and `create_text` numeric boundaries all passed. Only the five explicit-empty-name checks failed, and the independent `node_rename` discriminator confirmed native normalization. On replacement channel `4mor`, all five corrected explicit-empty calls were refused at MCP input validation with the page holding at 59 descendants after each, and all five omissions succeeded with defaults `Rectangle`, `Frame`, `Text`, `Frame`, and `Component 1`. Cleanup removed the disposable root and restored the same 59-descendant baseline.

### Functional gaps

#### F78-01 — `create_component` can leave an out-of-scope orphan

**Evidence.** The dispatcher invoked `validateSingleNodeWrite` without the available instance-interior refusal option at [`figma_plugin/src/main.ts:742`](../../figma_plugin/src/main.ts#L742), then the handler called `figma.createComponent()` before the fallible parent insertion at [`componentHandlers.ts:990`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/componentHandlers.ts#L990). Live on channel `o4g6`, converting the nested frame `I1396:121;1395:119` caused Figma to reject `insertChild` because the destination was inside an instance. Component `1396:123` survived at that time with `parent: null`, `removed: false`, and an empty path; `node_delete` refused it as outside the editable scope. A later `a7ps` read returned that ID in `missingNodeIds`, so the historical orphan is no longer present and no cleanup action remains.

**Required closure.** Refuse an instance or instance-interior source through the registered dispatcher before `figma.createComponent()` runs. Add a real-dispatch regression proving zero creation, then repeat the live probe and confirm no survivor exists.

#### F78-02 — creator cleanup failure is not truthfully surfaced

**Evidence.** `removeUncommitted()` returns `false` when removal is unavailable or throws ([`nodeUtils.ts:247`](../../figma_plugin/utils/nodeUtils.ts#L247)), but the six ordinary creator call sites ignore that result; [`nodeCreators.ts:168`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/nodeCreators.ts#L168) and [`componentHandlers.ts:322`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/componentHandlers.ts#L322) are representative. A deterministic append-failure plus remove-failure probe preserved the initiating `APPEND_FAILURE` but left the created node on the implicit page rather than the verified parent, with `removed: false` and no partial-mutation details. `create_component` has the opposite failure mode: its catch calls `component.remove()` directly at [`componentHandlers.ts:1083`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/componentHandlers.ts#L1083), so an injected cleanup throw replaced the original configuration error and bypassed partial disclosure. The current cleanup regression ([`v2.3.3.phase8.test.ts:684`](../../src/mcp_server/tests/unit/figma_plugin/v2.3.3.phase8.test.ts#L684)) checks logging and error precedence, not the survivor's placement or client-visible disclosure.

**Required closure.** Treat removal outcome as data on every creator path: preserve the initiating error, inspect whether the node actually disappeared, and attach `partialMutation`, survivor ID, and observed location whenever it did not. Regress append/configuration failure crossed with cleanup returning `false` or throwing through the registered command boundary.

#### F78-03 — component-set restoration failure masks and under-reports mutation

**Evidence.** `create_component_set` renames every source component before combining, then restores names with unguarded setters in its catch ([`componentHandlers.ts:1294`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/componentHandlers.ts#L1294)). A deterministic combine failure plus restoration-setter failure replaced the combine error, stopped later restoration, and left variant names applied without partial-mutation details. Existing tests cover successful restoration and the removed-member skip, not a present node whose restoration setter throws.

**Required closure.** Restore each member best-effort, preserve the initiating error, and disclose applied, restored, and unrestored names. Add a multi-member regression in which an early restoration setter fails while later members are still attempted.

#### F78-04 — `annotation_list` violates its node-and-subtree contract

**Evidence.** The registered description promises a node “and subtree” ([`annotation.ts:115`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/annotation.ts#L115)), but node mode rejects the root when the root itself lacks `annotations` before the descendant traversal begins ([`annotationHandlers.ts:109`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/annotationHandlers.ts#L109)). Live on `o4g6`, a `GROUP` root containing an annotated rectangle returned `UNKNOWN_ERROR: Node type GROUP does not support annotations` instead of the descendant.

**Required closure.** Permit any traversable root and test the annotation mixin inside the recursive collector. Regress at least a `GROUP` root with an annotated descendant through the registered callback and live tool.

#### F78-05 — `node_set_effects` silently loses cross-variant intent

**Evidence.** `node_set_effects` uses one shared object schema and allows `showShadowBehindNode` for every effect type ([`node.ts:633`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/node.ts#L633)). The plugin then deletes that field from `INNER_SHADOW` ([`stylingHandlers.ts:336`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/stylingHandlers.ts#L336)). Live on `o4g6`, the schema accepted that combination and the successful result silently omitted the requested field.

**Required closure.** Give `node_set_effects` a strict per-variant discriminated union, including a `DROP_SHADOW`-only `showShadowBehindNode`, and add negative schema-boundary and live regressions.

#### F78-06 — effect schemas omit live runtime numeric bounds

**Evidence.** Shadow/blur radii and GLASS controls are unconstrained `z.number()` fields in [`style.ts:103`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/style.ts#L103), [`style.ts:126`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/style.ts#L126), and [`style.ts:216`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/style.ts#L216). Live calls passed schema validation and were then rejected by Figma for `LAYER_BLUR.radius: -1`, `GLASS.radius: -1`, and `GLASS.lightIntensity`, `refraction`, and `dispersion` set to `2`.

**Required closure.** Constrain the live-confirmed ranges at the MCP boundary: the tested radii are non-negative, and `lightIntensity`, `refraction`, and `dispersion` are within `0..1`. Add schema tests using the exact rejected values and live-valid boundary values.

#### F78-07 — `create_instance.componentId` is not advertised

**Evidence.** The handler returns the resolved component ID at [`componentHandlers.ts:308`](../../figma_plugin/handlers/componentHandlers.ts#L308), and live `o4g6` calls returned it correctly. The registered output schema declares only `id`, `name`, and `parentId` ([`create.ts:227`](../../src/mcp_server/tools/create.ts#L227)). The output test even supplies `componentId` ([`outputSchema.test.ts:332`](../../src/mcp_server/tests/unit/tools/outputSchema.test.ts#L332)), but `looseOutput` accepts it as an undeclared extra and masks the drift.

**Required closure.** Declare and describe `componentId` in the emitted output schema, then assert its presence in `tools/list` rather than only safe-parsing a loose result.

#### F78-08 — removed component-set members were treated as clean recovery

**Evidence.** Re-auditing the new best-effort restoration exposed a pre-existing adverse path: when `combineAsVariants` removes a source component and then throws, recovery skipped that member because `removed === true`. If every still-present member's name restored, the handler rethrew the combine error without `partialMutation`, even though a pre-existing component had disappeared. The Phase 8 removed-member regression asserted only that later names restored.

**Required closure.** Preserve the combine error, continue restoring live members, classify removed members separately, and disclose the operation as partial whenever `removedComponents` is non-empty. The regression must assert the removed member plus the applied/restored/unrestored buckets.

#### F78-09 — unknown recovery state could authorize destructive cleanup

**Evidence.** The first remediation read `sourceFrame.removed` and each original child's `parent` through a fallback that converted an unreadable getter into `false`. Recovery could therefore conclude that the source was live and that the component owned no original children, remove the new component, and report a clean failure even though the component might still contain pre-existing user content.

**Required closure.** Treat source removal and child ownership as explicit live/removed/unknown states. Remove the component only after positive proof that the source is live, every original child is back on it, and the component is empty. Unknown, relocated, or still-owned children must keep the component and appear in partial-mutation evidence.

#### F78-10 — child restoration stopped after the first failure

**Evidence.** The recovery loop gated every later restoration on one shared `restoredAllChildren` flag. An early setter/insertion failure therefore prevented independent later children from being restored, even when those later moves were safe, and the one-child regression could not reveal the short circuit.

**Required closure.** Attempt every eligible child independently, retain original sibling order around children that never moved, preserve the initiating conversion error, and disclose each failed restoration without suppressing later attempts.

#### F78-11 — failed combine recovery ignored component placement

**Evidence.** `combineAsVariants` may reparent live components into a newly created component set and then throw. The recovery code compared only names and `removed`; if names restored and every component remained live, it rethrew a clean combine error even though the inputs no longer had their original parents and a set survived.

**Required closure.** Snapshot original parent IDs before the first rename. After any failed combine, compare every live component's current parent and include `reparentedComponents` or `unverifiedPlacementComponents` in a partial-mutation error whenever placement did not return to the captured state.

#### F78-12 — post-combine evidence reads could erase mutation truth

**Evidence.** After `combineAsVariants` returned, the handler read `name`, `id`, `parent`, and `children` outside a total recovery/projection boundary. A getter failure could replace a requested-rename error or surface as an ordinary clean failure after the set already existed. The handler also trusted, rather than verified, that the returned set used the requested parent.

**Required closure.** Snapshot the created set's required identity/name/location with guarded reads, verify its actual parent against the verified destination, and reuse those snapshots in every partial error. Treat required-field or location uncertainty as partial; make optional `variantGroupProperties`/child-count reads best-effort warnings that cannot erase a confirmed successful mutation.

#### F78-13 — effect `blendMode` was not schema-bounded

**Evidence.** Shadow and NOISE schemas used `z.string()` for `blendMode`, even though the pinned Figma typings define a closed 19-literal `BlendMode` union. `style_manage` and `node_set_effects` both accepted `NOT_A_BLEND_MODE`, and `normalizeEffects` forwarded it to Figma.

**Required closure.** Reuse one exact `BlendMode` enum across every effect variant, pin its literal inventory to the installed typings, accept every ratified value, and reject an invalid value through both registered effect surfaces.

#### F78-14 — failed-combine recovery could corrupt a surviving component set

**Evidence.** `combineAsVariants` may reparent the inputs into a durable `COMPONENT_SET` and then throw. The first recovery implementation restored every live member's original name before interpreting the new parent, turning valid variant names such as `Size=A` back into ordinary names such as `A` inside the surviving set. Its regression encoded that corrupted outcome. A sibling path treated an unreadable new-parent `type` as permission to perform the same unsafe restoration.

**Required closure.** Inspect placement before any recovery name write. Members confirmed inside a surviving component set retain or best-effort confirm their computed variant names; a changed parent whose type is unreadable blocks original-name restoration. Disclose surviving sets plus retained/unconfirmed variant members, and keep unknown placement partial.

#### F78-15 — explicit empty creator names cannot be preserved

**Evidence.** The first remediation replaced truthiness defaults with presence-sensitive assignments and its stubbed tests expected `""` to survive. The live `2476` matrix disproved that premise across all five surfaces: `create_shape` read back as `Rectangle`, `create_frame` as `Frame`, `create_text` as its text content (`empty-name` in the probe), `create_svg` as `Frame`, and `create_component_set` as `Component`. A separate `node_rename({name:""})` probe also read back as `Rectangle`, demonstrating native Figma normalization rather than stale plugin code.

**Required closure.** Reject an explicitly empty `name` on `create_shape`, `create_frame`, `create_text`, and `create_svg`, and an explicitly empty `componentSetName` on `create_component_set`, at both the MCP schema and plugin defense-in-depth boundaries before any creation, rename, or combine. Omission remains valid and retains each established default. Pin rejection/no-mutation and omission/default behavior across all five surfaces, then repeat those refusals live.

**Resolution.** The five registered MCP schemas now require `minLength:1`, and the plugin independently rejects exact-empty supplied names before any creator or combine call; omission and non-empty whitespace retain their prior meaning. Official-SDK live verification on channel `4mor` passed all five refusals with per-call descendant counts unchanged at 59, then passed all five omission paths with defaults `Rectangle`, `Frame`, `Text`, `Frame`, and `Component 1`. Cleanup deleted the disposable root and restored the 59-descendant baseline.

#### F78-16 — unreadable component removal state was classified as live

**Evidence.** Failed component-set recovery read `component.removed` through a boolean fallback. A throwing or non-boolean getter therefore became `false`, allowing later recovery logic to act as though the pre-existing component were confirmed live and omitting the uncertainty from the partial-mutation evidence.

**Required closure.** Model removal as `live | removed | unknown`. Unknown must never authorize a destructive or otherwise optimistic recovery inference and must be disclosed separately as `unknownRemovalComponents`.

#### F78-17 — survivor location evidence confused unknown with detached

**Evidence.** Ordinary creator cleanup mapped both `node.parent === null` and a throwing `node.parent`/`parent.id` getter to `survivingParentId:null`, then described either case as `detached/null`. The hostile-getter regression pinned the false claim rather than detecting it.

**Required closure.** Carry `survivingParentState: "located" | "detached" | "unknown"` alongside the nullable ID. Only an observed `parent === null` is detached; unreadable parent or ID state is unknown, and recovery prose must say so. Apply the analogous state field to `create_component`.

#### F78-18 — error reporting was not total for hostile thrown values

**Evidence.** Plugin `getStructuredError` directly read `code`, nested `error`, `message`, and `details`; the registered MCP callback wrapper repeated those reads and also called `String(error)`. A thrown Proxy whose getters and coercion throw could therefore replace the initiating mutation error, erase partial/survivor evidence, or make the tool callback reject instead of returning the promised error envelope.

**Required closure.** Guard every direct/nested property read, optional-details copy, and stringification. A hostile thrown value must still resolve to the canonical structured `UNKNOWN_ERROR` envelope, while a readable coded error continues to pass through structurally.

#### F78-19 — annotation recovery invented certainty when count readback failed

**Evidence.** After an annotation setter threw, a failed post-state read left `afterCount` equal to `beforeCount` and reported no partial mutation even when the append could have committed. The outer recovery path did the inverse: it converted any count-read failure to `0`, which could manufacture a false count delta and partial-mutation claim.

**Required closure.** Represent both counts as verified observations rather than guessed numbers. Rows use nullable `beforeCount`/`afterCount` plus required verification flags; an attempted append with unreadable post-state fails safe with `partialMutation:true`, `outcomeUnknown:true`, initiating `error`, recovery guidance, and optional secondary `postStateError`.

#### F78-20 — `create_text` silently accepted unsupported typography inputs

**Evidence.** `fontSize` accepted any number although the pinned Figma contract requires a minimum of 1; callback `||` defaults rewrote explicit zero; and the handler's broad font block swallowed load/assignment failures before returning success. `fontWeight` likewise accepted arbitrary numbers while `getFontStyle` recognizes only the nine 100-through-900 weights and silently mapped every off-grid value to `Regular`.

**Required closure.** Enforce `fontSize >= 1` and the exact supported weight inventory at the MCP boundary, default only omission, and let font-loading or font-size assignment failures flow through Phase 8 cleanup/partial-disclosure rather than returning a false success. Regress zero, negative, off-grid, valid boundary, and post-placement font failures.

### Testing gaps

#### T78-01 — catchall inventory has no independent oracle

**Evidence.** The Phase 7 test imports `INTENTIONAL_INPUT_CATCHALL_PATHS` from production, uses it both as the skip set and as the expected inventory, and therefore updates its own expectation whenever production adds an exemption ([`v2.3.3.phase7.test.ts:291`](../../src/mcp_server/tests/unit/figma_plugin/v2.3.3.phase7.test.ts#L291)). This does not mechanically enforce the “closed, test-pinned” decision.

**Required closure.** Pin the two ratified literal paths independently in the test and compare production plus emitted schemas against that fixed oracle.

#### T78-02 — the all-creators parent test omits two creators

**Evidence.** The test titled “every creator reports the parent” exercises six creators and omits `create_component` and `create_component_set` ([`v2.3.3.phase8.test.ts:428`](../../src/mcp_server/tests/unit/figma_plugin/v2.3.3.phase8.test.ts#L428)). Current source and live happy paths do return `parentId`, so this is a coverage gap rather than a current functional failure.

**Required closure.** Cover all eight creators and assert both handler output and emitted output-schema properties.

#### T78-03 — name-description conformance is under-pinned

**Evidence.** The conformance test accepts either `node_info` or `style_list` for every verification field and asserts only that more than 25 fields were visited ([`v2.3.3.phase5-6.remediation.test.ts:86`](../../src/mcp_server/tests/unit/figma_plugin/v2.3.3.phase5-6.remediation.test.ts#L86)). It can therefore pass after losing fields or after mapping a node-name field to the style read tool.

**Required closure.** Pin the exact 35 field paths and their expected read tool: node fields to `node_info`, and the style-delete verification field to `style_list`.

#### T78-04 — the shared creator-recovery helper lacked a complete caller matrix

**Evidence.** The first remediation drove cleanup-failure disclosure for `create_shape` and separately for `create_component`, but not for `create_frame`, `create_text`, `create_svg`, `create_instance`, or `node_clone`. Their cleanup-success tests would still pass if a caller stopped using the shared truthful-recovery helper.

**Required closure.** Independently inventory all six ordinary creators and inject a primary configuration failure plus cleanup failure for each, asserting the preserved primary error, `partialMutation`, survivor identity, actual parent, verified parent, and durable survivor state.

#### T78-05 — guide resources lacked semantic parity coverage

**Evidence.** The resource suite read only the constraints resource and pinned one heading. The server serves four guide resources directly from Markdown; there was no test proving all four responses exactly matched their source or retained the new creator/component-set/annotation/effect recovery semantics.

**Required closure.** Read all four resources, compare each response byte-for-byte with its Markdown source, and pin concise semantic markers for survivor disclosure, component-set recovery, traversable annotation roots, required parents, and effect bounds.

#### T78-06 — the NOISE variant oracle failed for two reasons

**Evidence.** The MONOTONE-plus-`opacity` negative fixture also supplied an effect color without required alpha and asserted only `success === false`. The test therefore stayed green if the `opacity` cross-variant rule regressed, because the unrelated RGBA error still rejected the input.

**Required closure.** Supply a fully valid RGBA color and assert the exact `properties.effects.0.opacity` issue path and variant-specific recovery message.

#### T78-07 — the direct-parent fixture did not model Figma's returned location

**Evidence.** The final full-suite run reached the new post-combine location assertion through `currentPageElimination.coverage.test.ts`, but that legacy mock returned a component set with no `parent` even while asserting that `combineAsVariants` received the verified parent. Production correctly treated the detached result as a partial mutation; the fixture contradicted the Figma placement behavior under test.

**Resolution.** The mock now returns the set under the exact `pageArg` supplied to `combineAsVariants`, so the test verifies both the call argument and the observable returned location.

#### T78-08 — the cleanup fixture omitted mandatory host-state evidence

**Evidence.** The final full-suite run reached the positive-proof `create_component` recovery boundary through `phase3.test.ts`, but the legacy mock source had no `removed` state and the mock component had no `children` array. The hardened recovery correctly classified both reads as unknown and refused destructive cleanup, while the old test expected unconditional removal.

**Resolution.** The fixture now supplies `source.removed: false` and an empty component `children` array, proving the source is live and the new component is empty before asserting successful cleanup. The suite title was also corrected from the absolute “No Orphans” wording to the success-containment/truthful-survivor contract.

#### T78-09 — the surviving-set oracle required corruption

**Evidence.** The failed-combine regression asserted that members already reparented into a surviving `COMPONENT_SET` ended with their original `A`/`B` names. That expectation made the test green only when recovery violated the variant naming contract.

**Required closure.** Require `Property=Value` member names after the throw, assert that no original-name write occurs, and cover both a readable surviving-set type and a throwing `parent.type` getter.

#### T78-10 — creator-name tests encoded a false host oracle

**Evidence.** Rev 51 added explicit-empty versus omitted coverage, but its Figma stubs allowed a node name to remain `""`. The live host normalized every empty creator/set name, so the green matrix proved only the local stub behavior and could not establish the claimed contract.

**Required closure.** Exercise the real handlers and official-SDK registered callbacks for all affected creators. Require schema refusal and plugin defense-in-depth refusal before the creator/combine function is called, while retaining each established omission default. The live matrix must verify the same zero-mutation refusal after the rebuilt plugin is loaded.

**Resolution.** Registered-callback tests now prove SDK `-32602` validation, zero dispatch, whitespace acceptance, and unchanged omissions; real-handler tests independently prove zero creator/combine calls for the same exact-empty inputs. The channel `4mor` official-SDK matrix supplied the missing live zero-mutation and omission-default evidence.

#### T78-11 — the hostile-parent oracle encoded false location evidence

**Evidence.** The cleanup-survivor test deliberately made the parent getter throw but still expected `survivingParentId:null`, with no state that could distinguish the unreadable parent from an observed detach.

**Required closure.** Require `survivingParentState:"unknown"` for unreadable parent/id getters, `"detached"` only for observed null, and `"located"` with the exact ID for a readable parent.

#### T78-12 — hostile thrown values never crossed the real error boundaries

**Evidence.** Existing error tests covered ordinary `Error` and plain coded objects only. None exercised a Proxy that throws from `code`, `error`, `message`, `details`, enumeration, and string coercion after a mutation or through a real registered callback.

**Required closure.** Drive the hostile value through plugin partial disclosure and the official-SDK callback boundary. Assert that evidence survives when available, optional hostile details are omitted, and the callback resolves in-band with `UNKNOWN_ERROR` instead of rejecting.

### Documentation errors

#### D78-01 — the previous closure statement was no longer current

The moved decision block says “Nothing from the Phase 7–8 review remains unfixed.” That was a dated Rev 49 claim, but it was presented at the top of the live task ledger after this re-verification disproved it. This changelog resolves the documentation error by placing that text in an explicitly historical section and making this finding ledger the current release status.

#### D78-02 — catchall/Q35 prose contradicts the implemented decision

The operative PRD still says the inventory contains `paint`, `effects`, and `layoutGrids`, later says “three entries,” and describes Q35 as open (`prd.md:174,183`) even though the adjacent resolution says effects left the inventory. The Phase 7 task repeats the three-entry wording before recording two (`task.md:139,141`). Maintainer-facing comments still say “three” or describe effects as catchall-backed (`src/mcp_server/tools/index.ts:64`, `src/mcp_server/tools/style.ts:6–10`), and the test title says “exactly the three” (`v2.3.3.phase7.test.ts:316`). Production currently has two exemptions.

**Required closure.** Reconcile every operative statement and title to the two-entry, Q35-resolved state while retaining the dated history as history.

#### D78-03 — Phase 8 cleanup ledger is stale

The Phase 8 unit-test item remains checked while labeled `[partial]`, saying the creator cleanup suites are outstanding (`task.md:172` after this section move). Those suites are present in the current Phase 8 test file. This wording now misstates both completion and the actual remaining problem: the uncovered cleanup-*failure outcome* in F78-02.

**Required closure.** Replace the stale coverage list with the precise unresolved adverse-path cases from F78-02/F78-03.

#### D78-04 — the containment/no-orphan guarantee was absolute

The PRD and task said no command may terminate while a created node sits outside its verified destination, and the current error playbook plus historical v2.3.2 changelog said creation failures never leave orphan nodes. F78-02 proves that `remove()` can throw or return without making `removed === true`; termination is unavoidable, so the truthful contract is an observable **success** boundary plus mandatory partial-disclosure on a cleanup survivor.

**Required closure.** Qualify G1/G3/D11 and the guides, record cleanup failure as an explicit residual, add a historical correction rather than rewriting the v2.3.2 entry, and teach reconciliation before retry.

#### D78-05 — component-set guide recovery predates required direct placement

The workflow calls `parentId` optional, while the error playbook tells callers to omit it and recover from a post-combine reparent failure. Phase 5 made `parentId`/`parentNodeName` required, and Phase 8 passes the verified parent directly to `combineAsVariants`; the documented path no longer exists.

**Required closure.** Require parent discovery in the workflow and replace omit/reparent guidance with the current pre-combine recovery buckets and post-combine existing-set partial disclosure.

#### D78-06 — public safety details lag the repaired contracts

The safety manual and guides do not yet state that `create_component` derives its destination from a verified source and refuses instance interiors, that an annotation node root may be a non-annotatable traversable container, or that node/style effects use strict variant-specific fields and the live-confirmed numeric bounds. The catchall rationale also still said `normalizeEffects` rebuilt values after that behavior was removed.

**Required closure.** Reconcile SAFETY Part A/B/C/D, all four guide sources, and the maintainer comment; the resources are direct views of those Markdown sources, so no duplicate mirror edit is needed.

### Nitpick

#### N78-01 — historical revision numbering and ordering

The moved history initially preserved two entries numbered Rev 28 and the final reverse-ordered sequence Rev 49, 48, 47, 46, 45. This did not affect behavior, but it was confusing. The Phase 1–2 closure entry is now uniquely numbered Rev 30A, its references are updated, and revisions 45–49 are ordered ascending.

### Live hypotheses rejected — not findings

- Minimal `LAYER_BLUR` without `blurType` and minimal `NOISE` without `blendMode` both succeeded live; Figma/default handling makes those fields optional. No “missing defaults” gap is recorded.
- `GLASS.depth: 0` succeeded live. No `depth >= 1` constraint is justified by current runtime evidence.

## Decision status

> The text in this section was moved from [`task.md`](task.md) and is preserved as a dated historical record. Statements such as “below” refer to the implementation phases in that file. Historical closure statements are superseded by the current re-verification status above.

**Decision status.** All open questions Q1–Q21 are resolved; [open-questions.md](file:///Users/neozhehan/Git/figma-edit-mcp/documentation/v2.3.3/reviews/open-questions.md) is the decision record. Q15 (resolved 2026-07-10, Option C): **one release, numbered 2.3.3, containing all 14 phases below** (PRD Rev 19, Release identity block). Q16–Q19 (raised 2026-07-17 by the Phase 3–4 implementation review) were resolved 2026-07-18 (PRD Revs 23–26); Q20–Q21 (raised by the follow-up review's findings P4-4/P4-8) were resolved 2026-07-18 (PRD Revs 28–29). **Nothing blocks implementation.** **Phase 3–4 review remediation landed 2026-07-18 (PRD Rev 30):** every P3/P4 finding from [reviews/Phase-3-&-4-review.md](file:///Users/neozhehan/Git/figma-edit-mcp/documentation/v2.3.3/reviews/Phase-3-%26-4-review.md) — including the P4-1 release blocker (official-SDK clients rejecting the structured-error envelope) — is fixed and verified; Phases 3 and 4 below are checked off accordingly. The suite is green at 696/696, `check:types:plugin`/`check:suppressions`/`check:versions` all pass, and the plugin bundle rebuilds byte-identical. **Reopened 2026-07-18:** the Phase 5–6 implementation review ([reviews/Phase-5-&-6-review.md](file:///Users/neozhehan/Git/figma-edit-mcp/documentation/v2.3.3/reviews/Phase-5-%26-6-review.md)) raised **Q22–Q26**. Q22 (parent-name refusal code regime) is resolved 2026-07-18 (Option A, PRD Rev 31): the refusal becomes the coded `PARENT_NAME_MISSING`/`PARENT_NAME_MISMATCH` factory pair — the reopened Phase 5 handler item below carries the remediation. Q23 (duplicate-target rejection layer) is resolved 2026-07-18 (Option B, PRD Rev 32): rejection moves to Layer 1 as a schema `.superRefine()` (no new code), with the plugin check retained as uncoded defense in depth — the reopened Phase 6 duplicate item below carries the remediation. Q24 (text partial-mutation before-value) is resolved 2026-07-18 (Option A, PRD Rev 33): the text row carries `before: {fontName}` + `whatChanged` with the flag set only when the font fallback applied — the reopened Phase 6 disclosure item below carries the remediation. Q25 (batch row vocabulary) is resolved 2026-07-18 (Option A, PRD Rev 34): all four aggregators' rows converge on `nodeId`/`status`/`error`, renaming the instance rows — the reopened Phase 6 item below carries the remediation, with the CHANGELOG entry in Phase 13. Q26 (legacy count fields) is resolved 2026-07-18 (Option B, PRD Rev 35): the batch handlers expose **only** the shared envelope counts, every tool-specific duplicate dropped (no marked aliases) — the reopened Phase 6 output-field item below carries the remediation. **All of Q22–Q26 are resolved**; the review's remaining findings are remediation work under recorded decisions, and Phases 5–6 close when they land. **Phase 5–6 review remediation landed 2026-07-19:** every finding (P5-1–P5-4, P6-1–P6-12, S1–S5) and the six 2026-07-18 ratifications are fixed under Q22–Q26 (PRD Revs 31–35) and verified. Phases 5 and 6 below are checked off accordingly. (Version-bump surfaces and live probes remain in Phases 13–14.) **A follow-up adversarial critique (2026-07-22, [reviews/Phase-5-&-6-critique.md](file:///Users/neozhehan/Git/figma-edit-mcp/documentation/v2.3.3/reviews/Phase-5-%26-6-critique.md)) found thirteen deeper fault-path defects (C1–C13) that the green suite did not drive — all confirmed valid and now fixed and verified:** the text partial-mutation disclosure (C1 report-scope bug, C2 mixed-font mutation), best-effort progress delivery (C3), instance-override truthfulness and target-TOCTOU (C4/C5), early-progress legacy names (C6), empty-parent-name classification (C9), retry/appendable wording (C11/C12), the missing recovery test (C13), and real registered-callback + fault-path coverage (C7/C8). The suite is green at **741/741**, `check:types:plugin`/`check:suppressions` pass, and the plugin bundle rebuilds idempotently. C10 (nested Layer-1 strictness) is recorded as a Phase 7 dependency. **A 2026-07-23 spot-check on the Phase 3–4 review's own findings** confirmed three of them undersold: P3-4 (the suppression checker mishandled directive-lookalike text inside a *multiline* template literal — the per-line string-stripper had no cross-line memory), P4-4 (`channel_join` dropped `details` on both its join-error and get-connect-payload legs, despite Q20's "details is preserved" record), and P4-5 (the ten operational codes were plain `ERRORS` strings, not `REFUSALS` factories, and the plugin's `UNKNOWN_ERROR` fallback was a repeated inline literal). All three are fixed and verified: `check-suppressions.ts` now masks strings/templates with a whole-file state machine (multiline-safe, tested); `figma-client.ts` and `channel.ts` forward `details`/`errorDetails` on both legs (proven with a real fake-WebSocket end-to-end test, not just a mocked boundary); and the registry is unified per **Q27** (resolved 2026-07-23, Option B, PRD Rev 36) — operational codes migrated into `REFUSALS`, `ERRORS` re-scoped to the closed legacy surface, `UNKNOWN_ERROR` named once per side of the (deliberately unimported) plugin-bundle boundary. **A second 2026-07-23 spot-check found those first-round P3-4/P4-5 fixes themselves undersold** in four ways: two mechanical (the checker's directive regex was unanchored, so a directive-shaped substring later in a comment was a false positive; and two `UNKNOWN_ERROR` string literals survived at `connectHandlers.ts` and `channel.ts`) — both now fixed and verified — and two that are genuine decisions, tracked as **Q28** (the checker's hand-rolled comment lexer carried a regex-literal desync that could mask a real directive — a false *negative*) and **Q29** (D9's letter governs the server-side `.superRefine()` messages, which are authored inline). **Q28 was resolved 2026-07-23** (Option C, PRD Rev 37): `check:suppressions` is rewritten onto the TypeScript parser (`ts.createSourceFile`), deleting the hand-rolled masking lexer and closing the desync false negative, with a regression test replaying the repro. **Q29 was resolved 2026-07-23** (Option B, PRD Rev 38): D9's central-registry rule is scoped to the coded plugin refusals; the server-side `.superRefine()` messages are a schema layer held to D9's content bar (a new Phase 4 test asserts the name-verification refinements meet it), no production message changed. **Successive spot-checks (2026-07-24, `reviews/P3-4-P4-5-remaining-gaps.md`) found both remediations undersold across two rounds; both are now hardened (PRD Revs 39–40).** P3-4: the Rev 37 checker re-derived directive recognition with its own regex and missed a starred continuation-line block directive TS honors (Rev 39 delegated recognition to TS's `commentDirectives`/`checkJsDirective`); a further recheck found Rev 39 still *classified* by a `@ts-ignore\b` regex, missing prefix-suffixed forms (`@ts-ignorefoo`) TS honors, bypassing the description rule on `@ts-expect-error_`, and misflagging an expect-error whose description mentions `@ts-ignore` — Rev 40 branches on TS's own `directive.type` with fail-closed handling, locked by nine more differential fixtures and a red-proof. P4-5: the inventory test (which only proved registry shape) now types the registry, checks all ratified codes absent from `ERRORS`, checks operational + scopes recovery content, adds a TS-AST source-use invariant, and (Rev 40) forbids any object-literal `throw` in plugin source (catching the reconstruct/spread-override bypass), confines the `"UNKNOWN_ERROR"` literal to its two approved definitions, and requires connectHandlers to use the imported constant — every failure mode red-proofed. **All twenty-nine open questions are resolved.** Neither un-checks a Phase 3–4 item: the gate exists and passes on real source, and Q29 was a doc/superRefine reconciliation.

**Third Phase 5–6 closure recheck remediation (2026-07-25, PRD Rev 44):** the three newly reproduced, non-deferred defects are fixed. Stored target/scope references now reject `removed === true`; `instance_set_overrides` requires a readable main component and captures the authoritative original ID immediately before the final synchronous gate/swap; and all four batch handlers normalize arbitrary thrown values through the shared `describeError()` fallback so every non-success row remains nonblank and schema-valid. The live output description now calls `before` diagnostic evidence rather than a guaranteed restoring-write input. Deterministic regressions cover removed targets/scope, failed/null/stale main-component capture, and empty/raw/null throws across all four aggregators.

**Fourth Phase 5–6 closure recheck remediation (2026-07-25, PRD Rev 45):** post-mutation telemetry is now total and best-effort. `instance_set_overrides` notification failure cannot enter outcome accounting or erase its envelope, and progress-delivery diagnostics normalize hostile thrown values instead of trusting `.message`. Regressions inject a throwing notification after a real swap and a hostile Proxy from text/delete progress delivery, proving truthful rows/counts and a returned D7 envelope. The dormant server result interface is also aligned to the Q25/Q26 vocabulary so it no longer advertises removed fields.

**Phase 7–8 adversarial review (2026-07-25, PRD Rev 46).** The review raised **Q30–Q35**; Q30–Q34 are resolved and landed, Q35 is open. Q30 (Option A): the D10 category check is the coded `ANNOTATION_CATEGORY_NOT_FOUND` factory refusal — inventory now twenty codes plus the fallback. Q31 (Option A): `annotation_set`'s description carves out of R6's shared retry sentence, since append is not idempotent and a `failed` row may already have appended. Q32 (Option A): the D7/Q9 partial-mutation vocabulary extends to annotation rows and to `create_component`/`create_component_set`'s durable-change failure paths. Q33 (hybrid): every creator resolves its destination as the last `await` before creation (`create_instance`'s parent read moved after the component import, closing a 15 s window); the full synchronous parent-predicate re-assert is deferred with a recorded trigger. Q34 (Option B): the catchall inventory is a closed, test-pinned list. **Q35 is open:** `style_manage.properties.effects[]` is exempt although its handler normalizes the payload, so it fails the membership rule Q34 ratified. **Remediation from the same review — all landed 2026-07-25 (Rev 46):** `setMultipleAnnotations`'s empty-batch path now **throws** a Layer 2 refusal instead of returning an envelope-less `{success:false, error:"<string>"}` that violated D7 Layer 3 and failed its own advertised output schema (top-level `error` is the D9 envelope object) — `deleteMultipleNodes` was the reference shape (P6-5); all six creator cleanup sites route through the shared `removeUncommitted()` helper, so a throwing `remove()` is reported and swallowed rather than replacing the error that caused the failure (the C3/R14/R15 rule that recovery machinery may not become a second failure); the dead `ComponentSetPlan.containingPage` field is gone (the same-page *check* stays); `annotation_list`'s node-mode reshape is now recorded in the PRD's Compatibility posture and the Phase 13 CHANGELOG checklist; `annotation_set`'s `nodeName` description now matches the G2 "current exact name, passed back verbatim" convention the create tools use; D8's "same change" claim is corrected in place to mean the release boundary, not the phase commit; `check:plugin` now detects real staleness (rebuild vs. the pre-build bundle) instead of diffing against HEAD, so it no longer reports uncommitted work as "out of date relative to its TypeScript sources", and it restores the bundle rather than leaving the tree mutated; and Phase 8's failure/cleanup coverage now spans `create_frame`, `create_svg`, `create_text` (across its font awaits), `create_instance`, `node_clone`, `create_component_set`'s name-restore (including the removed-member skip), and a masking-cleanup regression. A dispatcher-level test also asserts `annotation_set`'s batch-wide name verification with zero mutation. **Landed 2026-07-26 (PRD Rev 49):** the two remaining review findings. (1) **One name-verification description form across every tool** — 35 fields carried nine different phrasings, only one of which named the read tool or said "verbatim"; they now all read "The ⟨object⟩'s current exact name, passed back verbatim from `node_info`" (or `style_list` for `style_delete`), with a conformance test that fails on any future drift. The P5-4 assertion that pinned an un-backticked literal was rewritten to assert the contract rather than the markup. (2) **Every creator reports its `parentId`** — see the Phase 8 item. **Q35 is resolved (Rev 47)** — see the Phase 7 strictness items. **Nothing from the Phase 7–8 review remains unfixed**; the `SAFETY.md` wording and the two D11 residuals are Phase 12 tasks, listed there.

**New error codes introduced by this release** (twenty-two plus the ratified fallback — the inventory per Q16, resolved 2026-07-18: Option A, amended through Rev 73; each follows the D9 convention — structured `{error: {code, message, details?}}`, message embeds its own recovery, playbook entry in Phase 12):
`PLUGIN_PEER_UNAVAILABLE`, `PLUGIN_PEER_AMBIGUOUS`, `CHANNEL_IN_USE`, `VERSION_MISMATCH` (Phase 9 socket origin); `CHANNEL_NOT_BOUND` (Phase 9 MCP-client origin, Rev 57); `PAGE_LOAD_FAILED`, `PAGE_SCAN_FAILED`, `PAGE_NOT_FOUND`, `TARGET_NOT_PAGE`, `PAGE_LOAD_TIMEOUT`, `DOCUMENT_SCAN_INCOMPLETE`, `VARIABLE_IN_USE` (Phase 10 plugin origin); `VARIABLE_NAME_MISSING`, `VARIABLE_NAME_MISMATCH`, `COLLECTION_NAME_MISSING`, `COLLECTION_NAME_MISMATCH`, `STYLE_NAME_MISSING`, `STYLE_NAME_MISMATCH`, `VARIABLE_SCOPES_MISSING` (Phase 4, D5); `PARENT_NAME_MISSING`, `PARENT_NAME_MISMATCH` (Phase 5, D6 — Q22, Rev 31); `ANNOTATION_CATEGORY_NOT_FOUND` (Phase 7, D10 — Q30, Rev 46); plus **`UNKNOWN_ERROR`**, the ratified legacy fallback for failures not thrown as coded objects (its playbook entry documents it as "no machine classification exists; the recovery is in the message text"). Codes are never derived from message prose; every coded refusal originates from the registry at the layer that can actually raise it: socket admission, MCP-client state, or plugin dispatch. The legacy failure surface stays on the fallback pending the [v2.3.4 PRD](../v2.3.4/prd.md).

Adds D5–D8 (required current-name verification for variable/style updates and variable creation; schema-required `parentNodeName`; honest batch success semantics with tri-state `status`; same-change contract-doc sync), Phases 4–9 renumbering, and the compatibility posture for the fail-closed tightenings. Release identity updated accordingly — v2.3.3 is no longer a no-behavior-change release.
- **Rev 3, 2026-07-09** — resolved open-questions Q1 (schema mechanism for D5's conditionally required fields): **Option B** — flat schemas, requirement stated in both field and tool descriptions, server-side `.superRefine()` with actionable errors, plugin fail-closed check retained as defense in depth. Recorded as the D5 mechanism bullet; Phase 4 updated to reference it. Q2–Q8 remain open in `open-questions.md`.
- **Rev 4, 2026-07-09** — resolved open-questions Q2 (Gap 3 verification plan): **Option A** — the omission and mismatch cases are verified separately, at the layer that enforces each. Schema-boundary tests (Phase 5) cover omission; the Phase 9 live probe covers mismatch; the plugin error keeps its "missing" branch as defense in depth for non-conforming clients. Recorded in D6; Phase 9's probe list updated. Q3–Q8 remain open.
- **Rev 5, 2026-07-09** — resolved open-questions Q3 (SAFETY.md G2 target wording): **Option A** — G2 becomes the universal existing-object rule ("no write against an existing object proceeds unless the caller-supplied current name matches; creation verifies the identified parent or collection instead"), published only after the Phase 7 audit of every write tool against it. Recorded in D8; Phase 7 updated. Q4–Q8 remain open.

In a safety gate the false-negative direction is the dangerous one (a hidden suppression means the type gate passes while a real error is masked), which is why the authoritative tokenizer — not a narrower hand-rolled rule (Option B) or more lexer patches (Option A) — was chosen; `typescript` was already a dev dependency, so no new dependency was added. - **Rev 38, 2026-07-23** — resolved open-questions Q29 (D5 `.superRefine()` message centralization; raised by a second spot-check on the Phase 3–4 review's P4-5 finding, which noted D9's text named the server-side superRefine messages as requiring central plugin-module definition while they are authored inline): **Option B** — D9 gains a layer-scope note: the `Operation Denied:` prefix and central-`REFUSALS`-registry rule govern the *coded plugin refusals* (D5 verification errors, D6 parent-name error — they carry a `code`, are dispatcher-thrown, recur across handlers), while the server-side `.superRefine()` messages are a *different layer* (Zod schema-boundary validation, no `code`, coupled to one schema's constraint) that legitimately co-locates with its schema — it cannot source from the plugin registry the server does not import at runtime (Q27) — carries no prefix, and is held to D9's *content bar* instead. The name-verification refinements already meet the bar (name the read tool, say "pass it back verbatim"); a new Phase 4 test asserts this so it cannot regress. D9's original "governs … the D5-mechanism `.superRefine()` messages" wording is corrected in place: it predated the server/plugin registry split and demanded something structurally impossible. No production message changed. Full unification (Option A, a server-side message module) and single-sourced text via `src/shared` (Option C) were the alternatives; B was chosen because a superRefine message *is* part of the schema definition it describes, so co-location is correct design rather than a shortcut. **All twenty-nine open questions are resolved; nothing remains open.**
- **Rev 39, 2026-07-24** — hardened the Q28 and Q27 remediations after a follow-up review (`reviews/P3-4-P4-5-remaining-gaps.md`) verified two residual gaps against ground truth (real `tsc` + `sourceFile.commentDirectives`). **P3-4:** the Rev 37 checker enumerated comments correctly but re-derived directive recognition with its own regex on the comment body, which did not match TypeScript's multiline grammar — it missed a starred continuation-line block directive (an `@ts-ignore` on a ` * …` final line, which `tsc` honors: a safety-relevant *false negative*) and over-rejected inert forms (`////`, non-final block lines). Recognition is now delegated to TypeScript's own `sourceFile.commentDirectives` (`@ts-ignore`/`@ts-expect-error`) and `sourceFile.checkJsDirective` (`@ts-nocheck`), so the gate matches the compiler by construction; six differential fixtures ground-truth it and the starred form is red-proofed. **P4-5:** the inventory test proved only the registry's shape, not the production-source invariants; it now types the registry (no `as any`), asserts every ratified code (operational + D5 + D6) is absent from legacy `ERRORS`, asserts operational messages carry actionable recovery content, and adds a TS-AST source-use invariant that fails if any ratified code appears as a string literal outside `utils/errors.ts` — mechanically enforcing "every live coded throw routes through `REFUSALS`". Both P4-5 failure modes are red-proofed. No production behavior changed beyond the checker's directive recognition; suite green. Recorded in the Q27/Q28 entries and D3.
- **Rev 40, 2026-07-24** — a third recheck of `reviews/P3-4-P4-5-remaining-gaps.md` found the Rev 39 remediations still incomplete; both now closed and red-proofed. **P3-4:** Rev 39 took directive *ranges* from TypeScript but re-*classified* them with a `@ts-ignore\b` regex, which does not match TS's grammar — TS recognizes the directive as a PREFIX (no word boundary), so `@ts-ignorefoo`/`@ts-ignore123` are active `Ignore` directives the regex missed (safety-relevant false negatives), `@ts-expect-error_` bypassed the description rule, and an `@ts-expect-error` whose description mentioned `@ts-ignore` was misflagged (false positive). The checker now branches on TypeScript's own `directive.type`, reads the expect-error description as the fixed-length suffix after the canonical marker, and fails closed on an unknown type; nine differential fixtures and a suffixed-ignore red-proof lock it. **P4-5:** three enforcement holes closed — a new AST invariant forbids any object-literal `throw` in plugin source (catching the reconstruct/spread-override factory bypass the literal scan missed); a new scan confines the `"UNKNOWN_ERROR"` string literal to its two approved definitions and the connect-handler test now requires the imported identifier (rejecting a re-hardcoded quoted literal); and `VARIABLE_SCOPES_MISSING` joins the actionable-recovery assertion. All three P4-5 regressions are red-proofed. Suite green (766); no production behavior changed beyond the checker's classification.
- **Rev 41, 2026-07-24** — closure-audit remediation of the Phase 5–6 adversarial critique's reopened findings ([reviews/Phase-5-&-6-critique.md](reviews/Phase-5-%26-6-critique.md), R1–R9). Six were adjudicated and land here; three (R4 aggregator test-matrix completion, R7 missing-`parentId` error text, R8 the C4/C9 regression groups) remain open remediation items. **R1 (Q24 amendment):** the Q24 record admits the capture-failure residual — when `getStyledTextSegments` is unavailable (test stubs only; the API is stable on a live mixed-font `TextNode`) the before-value degrades to a non-restorable `{mixed: true}` marker and the edit proceeds best-effort rather than blocking. No code change: the existing `captureFontSnapshot` fallback is now the ratified contract, not a defect. **R2 (instance TOCTOU, reopens the C5 resolution):** the second target resolution (`getValidTargetInstances`), which runs immediately before execution, is upgraded from existence+type to the **full prevalidation predicate set** — identity/ID, `INSTANCE` type, exact requested name, scope-root membership, and lock/locked-ancestor state — re-asserted once against the original request; any drift fails the whole command before `swapComponent()` runs. C5's "fail on disappearance/type-change" is the narrow case this generalizes. **R3 (Q25 enforcement, corrects the Q26 premise):** the four batch `results` schemas stop being `z.array(z.any())` and encode the Q25 row vocabulary (`nodeId` + `status` required, `error`/partial-mutation fields optional, additive per-tool keys via `catchall`), so the registered schema now rejects a drifted row at the protocol boundary; the registered-callback test is coupled to real plugin-handler output and red-proofed against a reintroduced legacy-only/`instanceId` row. This makes literal Q26's claim that "registered-callback tests assert the returned key set … so `looseOutput` cannot re-mask drift," which the audit found unmet. **R5 (ledger):** the Phase 6 Three-Layer-Boundary and Layer-1 items keep their `[x]` but carry an explicit "implemented pending Phase 7 (nested strictness)" label, so the checkmark is not read as the full D7 Layer-1 "unknown keys at any depth" contract. **R6 (wording, logged amendment):** the PRD/task "report/retry exactly the failed items" shorthand is corrected to "every non-success row (`failed` and `skipped`)", matching the already-corrected emitted tool descriptions. **R9 (progress vocabulary):** intermediate `node_delete` progress payloads rename `successCount`/`failureCount` to the shared envelope names `succeededCount`/`failedCount`, so Q26's "no second count vocabulary" rule reaches the progress channel, not only the final return. R2/R3/R9 are code changes with tests; R1/R5/R6 are contract/ledger text. **Twenty-nine open questions remain resolved; R4/R7/R8 are tracked as open remediation, not open decisions** (closed in Rev 42).
- **Rev 42, 2026-07-25** — closed the three remaining closure-audit remediation items (R4/R7/R8), all test/wording work under already-resolved decisions. **R7:** the `create_component_set` missing-`parentId` error now matches its corrected schema — it names `node_info` and describes an "appendable parent container" (not a bare "parent container") and asks for both the ID and the exact current name; a phase-2 assertion pins the text. **R8:** the two production fixes gain their required regressions — a C4 early-field-success/later-field-failure test proves the failure row discloses BOTH the main-component swap and the already-applied override field (`before.appliedFields`), and C9 present-empty exact-match/mismatch cases land for BOTH parent paths (ordinary creator via the `create_svg` dispatcher in phase 1, component-set via the `create_component_set` dispatcher in phase 2), each red-proofing the truthiness bug — a present-empty `parentNodeName` is a `PARENT_NAME_MISMATCH`, and an exactly empty-named parent is usable. **R4:** the aggregator matrix gains an annotation all-failed + skipped case (annotation was success-only), and the mislabeled "node-gone and not-TEXT" text test is split so the not-`TEXT` branch is the first/only item and actually executes (previously a leading missing node made the handler skip the frame). No production behavior changed beyond the R7 error string. Two Rev-41 test-acceptance items were also completed here so their findings close cleanly: R6 gains an emitted-`tools/list` assertion over all four batch descriptions, and R9 gains a delete progress-emission regression that rejects the legacy `successCount`/`failureCount` vocabulary. **All R1–R9 closure-audit findings are now remediated.** *(Superseded in part by Rev 43: a second recheck reopened R2 and R3 and raised R10.)*
- **Rev 43, 2026-07-25** — remediation of the critique's **second** closure recheck, which reopened R2/R3 and raised R10 against the Rev 41/42 tree. **R2 (reopened — fixed properly).** The Rev 41 gate re-asserted the predicate set but was not the last operation before mutation: the dispatcher then awaited `getSourceInstanceData`, and each target awaited its own `getMainComponentAsync()`, before `swapComponent()` — every `await` an event-loop yield in which the validated target can drift. Three changes close it. (1) The predicate set is extracted into a **synchronous** `checkTargetPredicates` (identity/ID — newly checked — plus `INSTANCE` type, exact name, scope membership, lock/locked-ancestor); it reads only resolved node state, so it can run in the same turn as the mutation it guards, and both gates share one definition. (2) The dispatcher resolves source data **before** the target gate. (3) `setInstanceOverrides` hoists every target's `getMainComponentAsync()` await, re-asserts the whole batch synchronously **after** the hoist and **before** the first mutation, and re-asserts each target immediately before its own `swapComponent()` with no intervening `await`. Pre-mutation drift throws **outside** the P6-5 envelope catch, so it surfaces as a Layer 2 refusal (structured error, no envelope, no mutation) rather than an all-failed envelope; drift detected mid-loop, after earlier targets mutated, stays a failure row so the D7 envelope is not lost (the C3 lesson). A new end-to-end suite drives the real dispatcher and injects same-object drift *inside* the awaited windows (during source resolution, during the target's own main-component read, an id-mismatch impostor, and a later target drifting during an earlier target's await), each asserting a command error and `swapComponent()` call count **zero**, plus a no-drift baseline proving the gate does not over-reject. **R3 (reopened — cheap fix + honest wording).** The row schema now requires a non-empty actionable `error` on every `failed`/`skipped` row (Q25's "actionable per-item reason", previously optional regardless of status), red-proofed. The remaining gap is recorded rather than overstated: Q26's claim that callback tests make `looseOutput` unable to re-mask drift is **corrected in place** to state the actual enforcement level — the advertised schema cannot be exact at the top level (the SDK validates `structuredContent` on `isError` results and cannot advertise a union, forcing optional fields + `catchall`), so a legacy-only top-level payload still validates; per-row Q25 conformance is schema-enforced, top-level envelope exactness is **test**-enforced, and a runtime exact-result validator is explicitly deferred. **R10 (new — accepted diagnostic residual).** D7's promise that "the restoring write can be composed directly from the error in one round trip" is **withdrawn**: no registered tool applies a per-segment font map (no segment-range write surface exists outside `setCharacters`' internal strategies) and none swaps an existing instance back to a main component by ID, so the before-values are truthful *diagnostic evidence*, not an executable recovery payload. Q24's "genuinely restorable" is corrected to a record/evidence claim. Building the write surface or automatic rollback is deferred as new write capability needing its own verification and safety review. **Twenty-nine open questions remain resolved; R2/R3/R10 were remediation and contract-honesty work under recorded decisions.**
- **Rev 44, 2026-07-25** — remediation of the critique's **third** closure recheck (R11–R13), which found three non-deferred defects after Rev 43. **R11 (same-object removal):** Figma preserves stored references after collaborative deletion, so `checkTargetPredicates` now rejects `removed === true` on both the target and editable scope root; dispatcher tests delete each during an awaited main-component read and prove zero swaps. **R12 (truthful instance before-value):** the Rev 43 hoist could stale an earlier target's saved main ID during a later target's await, and swallowed capture errors as `null`. The handler now requires a valid async main-component read during batch preflight, captures target 0 authoritatively followed by a whole-batch synchronous recheck before the first swap, and captures each later target immediately before its final synchronous gate/swap; capture failure/null refuses before that target mutates, and a multi-target regression proves the returned `before.mainComponentId` equals the state observed immediately before the swap. **R13 (actionable-row totality):** `node_delete` and `annotation_set` copied `error.message` directly, allowing empty or non-`Error` throws to produce a protocol-invalid Q25 row; all four batch handlers now use the hardened shared `describeError()` fallback, which returns a nonblank diagnostic for empty strings/errors, raw values, `null`/`undefined`, null-prototype objects, and hostile renderers. Real-handler/schema tests cover empty `Error`, raw string, and `null` across all four aggregators. The live `before` schema description is corrected to diagnostic evidence, not guaranteed restoring-write input. Rev 43's accepted R3 top-level looseness and R10 missing write surface remain explicit scope decisions, not silently claimed as implemented.
- **Rev 45, 2026-07-25** — remediation of the critique's **fourth** closure recheck (R14/R15), which found two post-mutation telemetry paths capable of losing the D7 envelope after Rev 44. **R14 (notification isolation):** `setInstanceOverrides` called `figma.notify()` after deriving a real result inside its execution `try`, then called it again in the catch; a throwing notification after `swapComponent()` therefore erased the result and rethrew. Both sites now use a best-effort helper whose failure cannot enter outcome accounting; a regression proves that a post-swap notification failure still returns one success row with coherent counts. **R15 (progress diagnostic totality):** `sendProgressUpdate` caught `postMessage` failure but interpolated `err.message`, allowing a hostile Proxy to throw again while the catch reported it. Progress diagnostics now use the total `describeError()` formatter; text/delete regressions inject that Proxy and prove that row cardinality, counts, mutation truth, and the final envelope survive. The dormant server-side `setInstanceOverridesResult` interface is aligned to the Q25/Q26 envelope and row vocabulary so it no longer advertises the removed `totalCount`/`instanceId`/`message` fields. These are implementation remediations under the existing C3/D7 decision, not new open questions.
- **Rev 46, 2026-07-25** — resolved **Q30–Q34**, raised by the Phase 7–8 adversarial review. **Q30 (Option A):** the D10 category check becomes the coded `ANNOTATION_CATEGORY_NOT_FOUND` factory refusal; inventory grows to twenty codes plus the fallback; Q23's uncoded exception does not transfer because a category ID is not payload-detectable. **Q31 (Option A):** `annotation_set`'s description carves out of R6's shared retry sentence — append is not idempotent and a `failed` row may already have appended, so the description itself teaches list-before-retry rather than deferring it to the on-demand guides; the boundary suite asserts the carve-out against the emitted `tools/list`. **Q32 (Option A):** the D7/Q9 partial-mutation vocabulary extends to annotation rows and to the two component paths whose cleanup can leave the document durably changed (`create_component`'s un-restorable children or already-removed source frame, `create_component_set`'s post-combine failure); the annotation before-value is captured pre-attempt in loop scope (R12's no-post-hoc-before rule, via the C1 pattern). **Q33 (hybrid):** every creator resolves its destination as the last `await` before creation — `create_instance`'s parent read moves after `importComponentByKeyAsync`, closing a 15-second window between the verified destination and the placement — while a full synchronous parent-predicate re-assert is deferred and the remaining dispatcher-to-handler hop is recorded as a residual with an escalation trigger. **Q34 (Option B):** the catchall inventory is restated as a closed, test-pinned list whose membership rule is "the handler forwards the object untouched", not "the field looks polymorphic"; recording it surfaced that `effects` does not meet that rule, tracked as **Q35** rather than changed silently. Two defects fixed in passing because the ratified work rewrote the same lines: an annotation failure row now always carries a schema-valid `nodeId`, and the outer catch no longer reads its before-count back after the failure. Suite green at 863; `check:types:plugin` and `check:suppressions` pass; the bundle rebuilds idempotently.
- **Rev 47, 2026-07-25** — resolved **Q35** (raised by the Q34 implementation and reproduced live on channel `x507`): `style_manage.properties.effects[]` leaves the strictness-exemption inventory, which drops to two entries (`paints`, `layoutGrids` — both genuinely forwarded untouched). Decided on the project's **Golden Rule**: the old `{type: string, blendMode?} + catchall` shape failed first-call correctness (the emitted schema named two fields, so a model could not compose a valid effect from the schema) *and* one-round-trip recovery (a wrong key was accepted and silently dropped, so no error existed to recover from). `effects[]` becomes a discriminated union over the seven pinned `type` literals with per-variant fields and gated secondary discriminators (`blurType`, `noiseType`); the inventory is pinned to `plugin-api-standalone.d.ts` by a parity test that follows type aliases and `extends` bases, on the `ANNOTATION_PROPERTY_TYPES` precedent; and `normalizeEffects` now fills defaults **on top of** the caller's object instead of rebuilding from a field list — which also fixes a latent bug where a PROGRESSIVE blur was silently downgraded to a normal one and NOISE/TEXTURE/GLASS lost every field. Handler-only (schema untouched) and enumerate-four-types-only were both rejected — the first leaves the model guessing with no error to learn from, the second removes working NOISE/TEXTURE/GLASS support. Breaking for callers who sent undeclared keys inside an effect (those keys never reached Figma); Phase 13 CHANGELOG carries a before/after example. Suite green at 878.
- **Rev 48, 2026-07-26** — recorded the **`SHADER` residual decision** left open by Rev 47. Live probing (channel `zgkx`) showed Figma's runtime accepts a `SHADER` effect the pinned `@figma/plugin-typings@1.125.0` does not declare, so Q35's enumeration makes it unreachable. The fix was measured rather than estimated — `ShaderEffect` enters the union at **1.131.0**, the only other Effect-variant delta is an additive `noiseSizeVector?`, the plugin type-checks against 1.131.0 with **0 errors**, and the Q35 parity test correctly goes red on the bump — and then **deferred to [v2.3.4](../v2.3.4/prd.md) Track 2 (D8)**, because the typings also generate `nodeFields.generated.ts` and the bump would change what `node_info` can read inside a release whose D4 scope guard forbids unrelated change. The escape-hatch alternative is rejected in both releases (it would degrade every ordinary validation error to a two-branch union error). Recorded in D8's Q35 addendum and in `style.ts`; no code changed. Also corrected in the same pass: effect colours now require `a`, after the live runtime rejected a `DROP_SHADOW` whose `color` omitted alpha — a schema that accepted it would have passed a call Figma refuses, the same first-call-correctness failure Q35 exists to remove.
- **Rev 49, 2026-07-26** — closed the last two Phase 7–8 review findings. **One name-verification description form.** The field that carries G2 to the model read nine different ways across 35 fields (`nodeName`, `parentNodeName`, `childNodeName`, `startNodeName`/`endNodeName`, `styleName`), and only one of them named the read tool or said "verbatim" — a first-call-correctness leak on the most common failure the safety contract has, since the guides state the rule but load on demand while descriptions are always in context. All 35 now read "The ⟨object⟩'s current exact name, passed back verbatim from `node_info`" (`style_list` for `style_delete`), pinned by a conformance test that walks every registered input schema and fails on drift. The P5-4 test that asserted an un-backticked literal was rewritten to assert the contract, not the markup. **Creators report their destination.** `node_clone`, `create_svg`, `create_instance`, `create_component`, and `create_component_set` returned no `parentId`, so D11's containment guarantee was unconfirmable from the response — live verification had to issue a follow-up `node_info` for each. All five now return it, and all eight creator output schemas declare it. Suite green at 880. The remaining Phase 8 output — G1's observable-boundary wording and the two D11 residuals (same-stack micro-transient; Q33 destination freshness) — moved from a Phase 8 hand-off box into explicit Phase 12 tasks, so the `SAFETY.md` edits are tracked where they will be made.

- **Rev 76, 2026-08-02** — [Change 27](#change-27-live-measured-effect-bounds-and-the-one-glass-rule-rev-76) declares four effect bounds Figma enforces but the schema did not, found by live probing on channel `f6ux`. `NOISE.noiseSize`, `TEXTURE.noiseSize`, and `TEXTURE.radius` are `0–100` because the host **silently clamps** above 100 rather than refusing (`101` and `100000` both store as `100`), so an unbounded schema reported success for a value the document never held — the Rev 74 rule applied to three more fields. `NOISE.density` is `0–1`. A second GLASS effect in one array is refused at the boundary rather than by the host as an `UNKNOWN_ERROR`. GLASS's existing bounds were probed at both ends and confirmed truthful.
- **Rev 74, 2026-08-02** — [Change 24](#change-24-change-23-review-follow-up-and-truthfulness-repair-rev-74) corrects the GLASS write boundary after live channel `4b9u` completed the missing read-back step: Figma accepts `depth: 0` at the setter but stores and returns `1`. The schema now requires `depth >= 1`, matching the pinned typings and preventing successful-but-normalized writes; the PRD, public changelog, safety contract, and agent guides distinguish handler-side field preservation from native-host normalization.
